# DECISIONS.md — Essay Down

Append-only log of decisions. Planning-time entries are numbered `#000`; build-time entries use the ids named in the PRD (`#002-outline-feel`, `#003-coach-prompts`, `#004-pdf-pipeline`, `#005-v1-acceptance`, `#006-mac-sync-smoke`, `#007-claude-cli-sandbox`, `#008-visual-baselines`, `#009-retro-approval`, `#review-N-r<k>`), written by reconciliation commits from accepted human-gate records (RUNNER-SPEC §2, §5).

## #000-lock-decisions (2026-09-04, planning; recorded by the principal from Michael's stated answers)

- Fixture essay: Michael supplies his own ~5,000-word essay as `docs/fixture-essay.md` (PREFLIGHT item). Task 0.5 uses it; the agent writes a neutral essay only if the PREFLIGHT item is marked "agent writes".
- Undo across the sidecar: Cmd/Ctrl+Z after "Use this" in Rewrite restores both the sentence and the sidebar's chosen variant (PRD §6.5). Michael accepted the default ("I'll figure out if that's not fine when I use it"); revisit only from real use, as a v1.1 backlog item if needed.
- Coach access to the writing room: context-only (PRD §4, task 5.3c). Full agentic access is out of scope for v1 and would require an explicit opt-in feature.
- Coach release: ships in v0.2.0 (Phase 5, after v0.1.0), never in v0.1.0.
- Stated fallbacks (not tasks): Linux on Electron if WebKitGTK parity fails (decided at 6.2r); PDF pipeline per #004; `claude-cli` provider removed if `claude -p` leaves subscription coverage.
- Reviewers: Claude Opus, GPT-5.6 Sol, Grok 4.6, all on subscription; no API keys anywhere.
- Name: working codename Essay Down; identifiers fixed (`com.savagesystems.essaydown`, `.essaydown.json`, config dir `essaydown`); a rename is a find-and-replace.

## #010-push-policy (2026-09-05, task 0.0; recorded by the principal from Michael's stated policy)

- `MLSavage/essaydown` is **public**. A GitHub ruleset blocks force-push and deletion on `main`.
- Push policy: branches (`phase/*`, `task/*`, `plan/*`) and `ci/*` refs are pushed freely from the host; `main` and `v*` tags are pushed only by Michael, by hand, per `docs/PUBLISH.md`. Containers never push (RUNNER-SPEC §2; PRD §9.12).
- Enforced on the principal's own tool use by `.claude/settings.json` (project scope) `permissions.deny`: `git push --force*`, `git push -f*`, `git push --delete*`, `git push*:*` (any explicit refspec), `git tag -d*`, `gh release*`. Consequence: the principal never pushes a refspec or deletes a ref itself; `scripts/gate.sh` (run by Michael on the host) is the only thing that pushes `ci/<id>/a<n>` refs and deletes them at `gc`.
- Reversal: delete the six lines from `.claude/settings.json` and the ruleset on GitHub; nothing else depends on them.

## #011-ci-minutes (2026-09-05, task 0.0; recorded by the principal from Michael's stated decision)

- The repo was made public to get **unmetered GitHub Actions minutes** for the three-OS matrix (15 CI gates, macOS runners are the expensive leg).
- Consequence for content: nothing personal enters the tree. `docs/fixture-essay.md` is **agent-written** (neutral topic, PRD task 0.5's fallback: the history of the fountain pen), never Michael's own essay. This supersedes the first bullet of `#000-lock-decisions` and closes the PREFLIGHT "fixture essay" item as "agent writes the fixture".
- Leak rule restated for a public repo (BUILD-DEFAULTS §8): scans cover the whole working tree and all history; `.evidence/`, `.ext/`, `.claude/settings.local.json` stay gitignored.

## #009-bootstrap-commits (2026-09-05, task 0.0; principal)

- Where 0.0's commits go: `phase/0` is cut from the seed `main` (`660f3aa`). The principal commits 0.0's work there in small atomic commits — `chore(0.0): <part>` for policy/checklist commits, `wip(0.0): <part>` for the parts in this order: Dockerfile + compose.yml + versions.env → entrypoints → generate-tasks.mjs + validate-tasks.mjs → gate.sh → ralph.sh → conformance suite → bootstrap.sh → CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md → .gitattributes/.gitignore. `ralph/bootstrap.sh` makes the final `task(0.0): bootstrap` commit (tool versions and EXPECTED_COUNT in the message, every 0.0 check quoted in the journal entry) and initialises `.evidence/state/` with 0.0 passed. Reversal at any point before Phase 0 runs: `git branch -D phase/0 && rm -rf .evidence` — `main` is untouched.
- Runner decisions taken while implementing RUNNER-SPEC 1.7 (each is the smallest reading of the spec that runs; none adds a feature):
  - **Lock.** The host is macOS, which has no `flock` binary; the one lock is `.locks/ralph` created with `O_EXCL` (pid + time inside) with stale-pid recovery. Same semantics, no dependency. [Amended by #review-0-r2 H4: the semantics are *not* identical — `flock(2)` is released by the kernel when its holder dies, an `O_EXCL` file is not, so a dead holder's lock survives and recovery is the manual procedure of 0.25 (RUNNER-SPEC §1).]
  - **Worktrees across the bind mount.** Git worktree metadata stores absolute host paths, so the repo is mounted in the container at `/work` *and* at its host path (`compose.yml`); nothing rewrites paths. Consequence: `ESSAYDOWN_ROOT` must be the absolute checkout path (the root `compose.yml` defaults it to `PWD` from the repo root).
  - **Principal completion signal.** An `interactive-principal` task or a plan request is complete when a commit on its branch carries `<promise>DONE <id></promise>` in its message (the transcript promise has no equivalent for a human-driven branch). The runner then applies the same stop-check and integration transaction.
  - **Superseded dependencies.** A gate that a resolved plan request superseded counts as a satisfied dependency. Every other dependent was rewired by the planning commit; the only tasks still naming it are the fix tasks that commit appended after the producer (the generator rewrites `X` → `Xh`, so a fix task written as depending on `X` depends on the superseded gate). The runner warns (`WARN plan.…`) when a pre-existing pending dependent was not rewired.
  - **Close-record drift.** RUNNER-SPEC §8.0 says every ref must "still equal" its intended OID; `main` advances at the next close and the next phase branch advances on its first integration, so branch refs are checked by ancestry (intended OID still reachable) and tags by equality. Doctor uses the same rule.
  - **Reviewer parallelism.** The three reviewers are detached child processes whose exit codes land in `reviews/<phase>/r<k>/<reviewer>/exit-code`; the runner polls those files (no event loop is needed, so the rest of the runner stays synchronous).
  - **Physical review attempts.** The generator expands only `review-set` (into `r0`) and `needsCI`; `r<k≥1>` attempts, `.g<n>` repairs and `.r<n>` approval retries are written as explicit physical tasks by the planning commit (fields `execution`, `reviewer`, `reviewSet`, `reviewAttempt` on reviewer tasks). Fixtures may pin `phase` on a raw task; the real PRD never needs to.
  - **Human gate CLI.** `scripts/gate.sh <id> --outcome ACCEPT|REJECT [--payload k=v]… (--note text | --file path)`; observation gates accept only ACCEPT. The record is `human/<id>/a<n>.md` with a header (recordTarget, gateKind, outcome, sha, payload) and the note as body.
  - **CI adapter.** `gh` + `git push` on the host behind one adapter object; `RALPH_CI_ADAPTER=fake` (conformance suite) records runs and refs in `.ci-fake/`. Artifact digests are per-artifact directory digests (sorted file paths + SHA-256 of each file), recorded in `run.json`/`accepted.json` and re-verified by every consumer.
  - **Version fields for release closes** are read from `package.json`, `apps/desktop/src-tauri/tauri.conf.json` and `apps/desktop/src-tauri/Cargo.toml` at the proposed commit (all present must agree with `releaseVersion`).
  - **Stop-check pre-0.1.** `ralph/stop-check.sh` exits 1 when a tree has no `package.json`, so task 0.1 cannot integrate until it produces the scaffold; 0.0 is the only task that skips the suite.

## #012-container-credentials (2026-09-05, task 0.0; principal, verified on the Mini)

- PREFLIGHT §0.5 assumed the host's `~/.claude`, `~/.codex` and `~/.grok` could be bind-mounted read-only into the container. Verified false for Claude Code on macOS: its login lives in the macOS Keychain, not in `~/.claude`, and the CLI writes to its config dir on every run. Grok's `~/.grok` also holds its binary and caches next to `auth.json`.
- Cut, not machinery: each CLI logs in **once inside the container** (`docker compose run --rm claude-login | codex-login | grok-login`, subscription accounts, no API keys) and its state persists in a container-owned named volume (`essaydown-claude-home`, `essaydown-codex-home`, `essaydown-grok-home`) mounted as that CLI's home directory. Nothing from the host's `~/.ssh`, `~/.config/gh`, `~/.claude`, `~/.codex` or `~/.grok` is ever mounted; `boundary-check` proves the git/gh side of the boundary and its output is quoted in the 0.0 journal entry. The 0.0 acceptance phrase "credentials mounted read-only" is therefore satisfied in spirit (the host's login state is never exposed) and not literally (the container's own login state is writable, because the CLIs rotate tokens).
- Reversal: `docker volume rm essaydown-claude-home essaydown-codex-home essaydown-grok-home` and log in again.

## #repair-0.1 (2026-09-06, principal; manual state repair, audited)

- Task 0.1 ran three attempts (turn budget, background install, turn budget) that each ended without a journal entry; the runner emitted `NO-JOURNAL` three times but left the task `running` with `attempts: 3` because journal-less attempts did not count toward the three-attempt limit (fixed in `fix(runner): NO-JOURNAL/NO-COMMIT attempts count toward STUCK`). Repair: the principal set 0.1 to `blocked` (STUCK after 3 attempts) through the state library under the lock, then `ralph.sh retry 0.1` (branch `task/0.1` kept with its three recovery commits, `attempts = 0`). Reversal: none needed; the branch and evidence are untouched.

## #013-actionlint-gap (2026-09-06, task 0.2; agent finding, not a Michael decision — cut per CLAUDE.md's "cut the feature to a manual procedure" rule, no machinery added)

- Task 0.2's acceptance reads "ci.yml lints under `actionlint` locally and `act --dryrun` resolves the three jobs" (PRD §8, and the same `actionlint` phrase recurs in 2.1, 4.0, 6.1, 6.2's acceptance text). Neither `actionlint` nor `act` is installed in the `essaydown-dev` image: `docker/Dockerfile` (task 0.0) does not install them, `docker/versions.env` has no version pinned for either, and Docker itself is not available inside the container (no docker-outside-docker), so even a locally-fetched `act` binary could not run anything beyond `--dryrun`.
- Adding either tool to the image is a new pinned dependency (parallel to how epubcheck/html-validate were pinned by 0.0), which CLAUDE.md's non-negotiables gate on a `docs/DECISIONS.md` note **and Michael's OK**; a `loop`-execution task has no channel to obtain that OK synchronously, and fabricating it here would violate "never write a DECISIONS entry attributed to Michael." So this task does not touch `docker/Dockerfile` or `docker/versions.env`.
- Cut, not machinery: `.github/workflows/ci.yml` was written and verified every other way available in-container — `pnpm lint && pnpm test && cargo test` green, hand-checked YAML (no tabs, consistent indentation, no parser dependency added to check it), and every `docker/versions.env` value the workflow reads (`NODE_VERSION`, `PNPM_VERSION`, `RUST_TOOLCHAIN`, `EPUBCHECK_VERSION`, `HTML_VALIDATE_VERSION`) matched by direct comparison. The `actionlint`/`act --dryrun` sentence itself is **not** verified by this attempt and is deferred to a manual procedure: before this task (or its CI gate `0.2h`) is treated as accepted, Michael either (a) runs `actionlint .github/workflows/ci.yml` and `act --dryrun` on his own host and reports the result, or (b) gives explicit OK to pin `actionlint`/`act` into `docker/versions.env` + `docker/Dockerfile` in a follow-up task, after which every later task naming `actionlint` in its acceptance (2.1, 4.0, 6.1, 6.2) can verify it in-container too.
- Reversal: none needed; no image or pinned-version file was touched.

## #014-act-dryrun (2026-09-06, task 0.2; principal, verified in the container)

- `act -n` (dry run) still connects to the Docker daemon to plan the `Set up job` step and skips the macOS and Windows matrix legs as unsupported; the container has no Docker socket by design (credential boundary, PRD §9.12) and mounting one is machinery. Cut: the **in-container** reading of "ci.yml lints under `actionlint` locally and `act --dryrun` resolves the three jobs" is `actionlint <workflow>` clean plus `act --list -W <workflow>` listing the jobs (it parses the workflow and matrix without Docker); the three-OS proof is the gate `<id>h` run itself. Applies to every acceptance that names `actionlint`/`act` (0.2, 2.1, 4.0, 6.1, 6.2). The agent's finding #013 (on the 0.2 branch) is resolved by `fix(image) befc9b2` and this entry.
- Reversal: none needed; no tool or mount was added.

## #repair-phase0-checkout (2026-09-06, principal; manual repair, audited here)

- After `task(0.1)` integrated (`4f4ace9`) by `update-ref`, the host checkout on `phase/0` kept its pre-0.1 index. The principal's next three chore commits (`befc9b2`, `29c6df3`, `9bf9eca`) were built from that stale index and deleted all 78 files of 0.1 from `phase/0`. Nothing was pushed and no task integrated on top; 0.2's integration rebase exposed it as a journal conflict. Repair: `git reset --hard 4f4ace9` on the host checkout, the three commits re-created from the correct base (`30373f1`, `c666d52`, `f375169`; same intent, five files), then `fix(runner): refresh a clean checkout on a branch the runner moved`. Rule from now on: the runner refreshes clean checkouts after every ref move and `doctor` flags staged changes on `main`/`phase/*` checkouts; the principal commits on the host checkout only when `doctor` is clean.
- Reversal: none; `phase/0` up to `4f4ace9` is untouched and the three dropped commits are reachable only from the reflog.

## #015-principal-rotation (2026-09-06, task 0.3 in flight; recorded by the principal from Michael's stated rule)

- **Rule.** At `HUMAN_GATE N.verifyh` the principal session rotates regardless of context: `/rotate` (handoff + `next-prompt.md`, committed under the runner lock), then "ready to rotate" with the relaunch command. A reconciliation (`N.12.r0d`) is never run by the session that ran the phase's tasks. Written into `docs/PRINCIPAL.md` (Handoff cadence).
- **Reminder, not machinery.** `ralph.sh run` prints `ROTATE-PRINCIPAL` on the line after `HUMAN_GATE N.verifyh`; `scripts/gate.sh` prints the same line after recording any `N.verifyh` attempt (ACCEPT or GATE-FAILED). It is an advisory line, not a stop signal (RUNNER-SPEC §11); a conformance test covers both emitters and the absence at other gates.
- **Session start.** `.claude/settings.json` gains a `SessionStart` hook printing the newest `docs/handoffs/*.md` path and `ralph.sh status`'s first line. `.claude/commands/rotate.md` is the procedure (numbering, headings, lock hold, watcher stop, the two relaunch lines).
- Numbered #015 because #012 (container credentials) was already taken when Michael named this "#012-principal-rotation".
- Reversal: `git revert` the `chore(0.0): principal rotation` commit.

## #016-passed-task-files (2026-09-06, task 0.5 blocked after three attempts; principal interpretation, reversible)

- **What happened.** 0.5 stopped three times without DONE on a rule conflict it read as: CLAUDE.md line 8 "Passed tasks are never edited" versus its own task text (add `fixtures/markdown/essay-fixture.md`, the 45th file), because 0.4's `packages/core/test/fixtures-corpus.test.ts` asserts the literal count `44` over the whole directory. The fixture, its canonical sibling and the `index.json` entry were finished on the first attempt.
- **Where the slogan lives.** "Passed tasks are never edited" is CLAUDE.md line 8, AGENTS.md line 8, PRINCIPAL.md line 21, and PRD §10 (lines 173 and 517: "passed tasks are never mutated", "rather than mutating passed tasks"). RUNNER-SPEC contains no such sentence; its §6 "reject deletion or mutation of any task that has left `pending`" is about records in `ralph/tasks.json` and runtime state. RUNNER-SPEC is silent on files.
- **Reading.** The slogan is about **task records, ids, status and integrated commits**. It does not freeze the files a passed task created; every later task changes earlier files as its text requires (0.6 "fix format.ts until green", 0.7 extends core), and the plan expects this corpus to grow (0.6's acceptance counts 45 fixtures).
- **0.4 was right at the time.** 0.4's acceptance said "44 files + 44 canonical siblings present" and "a hand-computed value written in the test"; the CLAUDE.md test rule reads counts from index files only "where the task says so". The agent wrote what its acceptance demanded: 44 was correct at 0.4 and became wrong at 0.5. No fault is recorded against 0.4.
- **What settles it.** CLAUDE.md step 2 and RUNNER-SPEC §4.3 require `pnpm lint && pnpm test && cargo test` green from 0.1 onward. 0.5 cannot both add the 45th file (its task text) and leave that assertion untouched (the green bar); the edit is compelled by the green bar, so it is inside 0.5's scope, not a "while I'm here" refactor.
- **Consequence for 0.5.** Replace the two `toHaveLength(44)` literals in that test with the relationship they stand for: every source `.md` has a `.canonical.md` sibling and an `index.json` entry, and the counts of the three sets are equal (the total derives from the directory's own `index.json`). Everything else in 0.4's test and its integrated commit `6a9acdc` stays untouched. No new task, no machinery; the 0.12 reviewers see the change in the 0.5 diff. The authorization also stands in `docs/lessons.md` under `[0.4]`, the agent's orientation channel.
- **How the retry sees it.** `startTask` (`ralph/lib/integrate.mjs`) reuses an existing `task/<id>` branch and worktree, so a retried agent never sees commits made to `phase/0` after its branch was cut, and a principal commit inside `task/0.5` would be squashed into `task(0.5)` and put a DECISIONS edit inside a fixture task's diff. Procedure instead: `git worktree remove .wt/0.5 --force`, `git branch -m task/0.5 abandoned/0.5-r1`, `ralph/ralph.sh retry 0.5`; the runner cuts a fresh `task/0.5` from the current `phase/0` and the agent rewrites the essay (agent-written filler, one attempt). RUNNER-SPEC is silent on renaming an unintegrated task branch; this entry, not the spec, sanctions it.
- Reversal: `git revert` this commit and append a `0.4.g1`-style fix task via a planning commit instead.

## #017-host-checkout-commit (2026-09-06, principal; manual repair, audited here)

- **What happened.** The principal's handoff commit `c8b234a` ("chore(0.0): handoff 003", 10:18:32 local) was made from the host checkout 57 seconds after the runner integrated `task(0.4)` `6a9acdc`, with a stale index. It deleted 91 files: all 90 fixtures in `fixtures/markdown/`, `packages/core/test/fixtures-corpus.test.ts`, 0.4's journal entry and its two lessons lines. A recurrence of the `[0.2]` lesson and `#repair-phase0-checkout`: the `76774f2` runner fix refreshes only a **clean** checkout. `git ls-tree -r --name-only phase/0 | grep -c '^fixtures/markdown/'` read `0`; rebasing `task/0.5` onto that `phase/0` failed (modify/delete on `fixtures/markdown/index.json`, content conflict in the journal), so the first STUCK 0.5 recovery plan would have ended at `CONFLICT 0.5`.
- **Repair.** Michael verified it independently on the Mini and committed `fix(0.4): restore the 91 files and 4 log lines c8b234a deleted` (`59d2d3e`) on `phase/0`. After it the same rebase is clean and the corpus test reads 45/45/45. `task(0.4)` `6a9acdc` itself was never rewritten; passed-task immutability holds.
- **Doctor gap (finding for the 0.12 reviewers, not fixed here).** `doctor`'s ancestry check cannot see a passed task's files silently reverted by a later commit: `6a9acdc` remained an ancestor of `phase/0` throughout. Recorded as a finding for the Phase 0 review set to dispose of; no machinery is added to `doctor` for it.
- **Manual procedure (prevents a third recurrence).** The principal never commits on the host checkout while the runner loop is live. Before staging anything on the host checkout: `ralph/ralph.sh doctor` must be clean; run `git reset --hard phase/0` (or `git stash && git reset --hard phase/0 && git stash pop` when carrying edits); confirm `git log -1 phase/0` names the commit expected; only then stage and commit. Manual procedure only.
- **Close constraint.** RUNNER-SPEC §8.1 requires `phase/0`'s head to equal `reconciliation_sha` at `0.close`. Every principal commit on `phase/0` after `0.12.r0d` integrates breaks the close: no principal commits on `phase/0` after that point.
- Reversal: none needed for the record; the repair commit is reversed by `git revert 59d2d3e` (which would re-delete the 91 files).

## #018-gate-0.verifyh-line-endings (2026-09-06, plan.0.verifyh.r0; principal, from Michael's reading of the run; scope choice per his guide's recommendation)

- **What happened.** `scripts/gate.sh 0.verifyh` a1 (ci.yml run 34037147355 at `f8c91c5`): ubuntu-latest 431/431, macos-latest 431/431, windows-latest 99 of 419 failed across 6 test files (`roundtrip` 90, `opaque-roundtrip` 5, `blocks`, `fixtures-corpus`, `markdown-style`, `sentences` 1 each), every failure `\r\n` versus `\n`. `GATE-FAILED`, `0.verifyh` → `blocked`, plan request `plan.0.verifyh.r0`.
- **Cause.** `.gitattributes` held one line (`docs/lessons.md merge=union`): no `text`/`eol` policy. `ci.yml` uses a bare `actions/checkout@v4`, whose Windows runner defaults to `core.autocrlf=true`, so every LF file was rewritten CRLF at checkout. Six tests read `fixtures/markdown/**`, `packages/core/test/fixtures/**` and `docs/MARKDOWN-STYLE.md` byte-for-byte; the index holds CR in exactly one path (`fixtures/markdown/crlf-line-endings.md`, by design), so on Windows the corpus was rewritten before the tests read it and the CRLF fixture stopped being the distinct case its test needs. Code/config-caused, not transient: no `gate.sh rerun` (RUNNER-SPEC §2 restricts `a<n>` to transient failures; any tree change is a `.g<n>` repair).
- **Scope decision (explicit, so the fix task does not pick).** Two candidates: (1) minimal, `-text` on the three path families; (2) repo-wide `* text=auto eol=lf` plus `-text` on the two fixture trees. Chosen: (2), one line broader and durable against the next file a test reads byte-for-byte (Michael's guide, 80%; principal agrees). Exact `.gitattributes` content, named in 0.13's text so the agent cannot widen it: `* text=auto eol=lf` / `fixtures/** -text` / `packages/core/test/fixtures/** -text` / `docs/lessons.md merge=union`. Index check before the change: 240 paths `i/lf`, 16 binary, 3 `i/none`, 1 `i/crlf` (the CRLF fixture, under a `-text` tree) — `text=auto` renormalises nothing that is tracked.
- **Graph change (RUNNER-SPEC §2 rule (a)).** Appended after `0.verify` in PRD §8: `0.13` (the `.gitattributes` fix, `sonnet`, depends on `0.verify`; zero new dependencies; Linux container ⇒ the in-container Windows-style checkout is a proxy, the only proof is the gate) and `0.verify.g1` (`needsCI`, same `ci` object with `refTemplate` `ci/0.verify.g1/a{n}`, depends on `0.13`); `0.12` now depends on `0.verify.g1`, so the generator rewires `0.12.r0a/b/c` to `0.verify.g1h`; `0.close` unchanged. `ralph/EXPECTED_COUNT` 122 → 125. At integration `sync-state` marks `0.verifyh` `superseded`. Next human gate: `scripts/gate.sh 0.verify.g1h`.
- **CI evidence path convention (second finding, settled here, no machinery).** The runner writes CI evidence under `.evidence/ci/<gate-id>/` (`0.2h/`, `0.verifyh/`; `ralph/lib/gate.mjs` `ctx.ciDir(t.id)`), i.e. `/logs/ci/<producer>h/a<n>/` and `/logs/ci/<producer>h/accepted/`. RUNNER-SPEC §3 ("fetches artifacts into `ci/<id>/a<n>/`") and §5.2–5.3 (`/logs/ci/<verifier_id>/accepted/`), the generated gate descriptions (`/logs/ci/<producer>/a<n>/`), `0.verify`'s `ciAcceptance` (`/logs/ci/0.verify/accepted/`) and the reviewer prompt spelled the producer id; §8.3 (`<verifier_id>h`, "read through its accepted.json") and §9 (`/logs/ci/<candidate_gate_id>/accepted.json`) already read the gate id. The spec contradicts itself and the runner chose the gate id; per CLAUDE.md the reading is recorded, not machined: **the gate id is the convention** — every `/logs/ci/<x>/accepted/` reference resolves to `<x>` = `<producer>h`. Of the two options Michael offered (record the reading, or fix the reviewer input path) the reviewer input path is fixed, because a recorded reading does not stop Grok from citing a directory that does not exist: `docker/entrypoints/essaydown-common.sh` (`review_prompt`) and `docker/entrypoints/grok-review` now derive `/logs/ci/${verifier_id}h/accepted/`. Left as they are, deliberately: the generator's gate-description string and `0.verify`'s `ciAcceptance` (changing either mutates the fingerprint of a passed task, which `sync-state` refuses), and the RUNNER-SPEC wording (the spec is reviewed as-is by 0.12; this entry is deviation 23 for the reviewers' list, with the request that the §3/§5 wording be aligned to the gate id in the reconciliation or the backlog). `0.verify.g1`'s `ciAcceptance` already names `/logs/ci/0.verify.g1h/accepted/`. No conformance test covers the entrypoint strings (they run only in the container); the check is the Grok report citing an existing directory at `0.12.r0c`.
- **For the 0.12 reviewers** (with #017's doctor gap): `docs/RUNNER-SPEC.md` §3/§5 producer-id wording versus the runner's gate-id evidence layout; whether a `.gitattributes` policy belongs in BUILD-DEFAULTS §9 as a bootstrap default (an acceptance that says "byte-identical" needs it before its first CI gate).
- **Reversal.** Before this planning commit integrates: `git -C .wt/plan.0.verifyh.r0 reset --hard phase/0` (branch back to its cut; the request stays `running`, which is the status `ralph.sh plan` left it in — no ralph command returns a request to `pending`, so the reversal is git-only). After integration a plan request has no undo: `0.verifyh` stays `superseded` and any further change is another planning commit.

## #review-0-r0 (2026-09-06, task 0.12.r0d; principal reconciliation of review attempt r0 at implementation_sha 81273f59de38f43d3f4e4347100a66a04cd41b4e, verifier 0.verify.g1)

verdict: FAIL

- **Reviewer outcomes.** Claude (Opus, cold build, probes): FAIL, 2 blockers / 5 should-fix / 5 nits. Sol (Codex, cold build, probes incl. a two-process runner probe): FAIL, 3 blockers / 8 should-fix. Grok (read-only, cites /logs/ci/0.verify.g1h/accepted/): returned PASS, 0 blockers / 8 should-fix / 2 nits. Reports byte-identical in `docs/reviews/phase-0-r0-{claude,sol,grok}.md`. Claude's "the corpus is the only oracle" and Sol's "manually maintained grammar" are what H3 and H2 address; property-based generation over the §6.1 node set is v1.1. Claude's and Sol's `cargo test` 0/0/0 is G13. Grok's F14 restatement stands as recorded.

F17's manual procedure is read as **"before the next container task that can observe the change"**, recorded here; no wording change to #review-0-r0. Observations, not deviations: the runner printed `ROTATE-PRINCIPAL` itself at 0.verify.r2h (0.18's code, first verifier gate since it went live; `gate.sh` printed the second copy); the principal's background-shell watcher was killed silently three times by the harness's memory-pressure guard during the r1→r2 chain and was replaced by a persistent `Monitor` poll (a host-side observation tool, not runner machinery; handoff 007 Gotchas) — the next principal checks `ralph.sh status` before trusting silence.

## #repair-1.9 (2026-09-09, principal; manual state repair, audited here; Michael's texts verbatim, Michael verified the design independently)

- **What happened.** `scripts/gate.sh 1.9` was run on 2026-09-08T07:18:15Z with the placeholder text of the prepared command unedited: `a1.md` and `accepted.json` carry `verdictText: "<your verdict on the outline idea>"` and the record body `<what you thought of the editor, the toggle and the Typora check>`; state moved `human-pending → passed` (ACCEPT, attempts 1, accepted_gate_attempt 1). The gate accepted a record whose payload and body were the template's own angle-bracket placeholders. No runner command re-records a passed observation gate: `requireEligible` refuses `passed`, `gate.sh rerun` is for transient CI failures only (RUNNER-SPEC §2), `gate.sh abandon` reads the CI directory, and `doctor`'s repair line for the human case names a `--resume` that `humanGate` does not implement (finding below).
- **Repair (cut to a manual procedure, no machinery).** With the runner idle (`.locks/` empty, no `ralph run` process) and `doctor` clean, the principal: kept `a1.md` and copied `accepted.json` to `accepted.json.a1-backup`; wrote `.evidence/human/1.9/a2.md` in the layout `humanGate` writes (`recorded_at 2026-09-09T05:59:10.836Z`, `sha ca1cd3d`, the head Michael was observing when he formed the opinion and the head `a1` names — the right value for a re-record of the same observation, not the current tip, which had moved by the docs-only handoff commit `5345198` that changed nothing he observed) with Michael's verdict as `payload.verdictText` and his record of the editor, the toggle and the reader check as the body, both verbatim; wrote `accepted.json` (`attempt 2`, `evidence_path .evidence/human/1.9/a2.md`, same payload and `recorded_at`); then under `withLock` set exactly the three fields `humanGate` sets on ACCEPT — `attempts 1 → 2`, `accepted_gate_attempt 1 → 2`, `finished_at → 2026-09-09T05:59:10.836Z` — leaving `status`, `outcome`, `task_branch`, `integrated_sha` unchanged (audit line `transition 1.9 passed -> passed (a2 ACCEPT; manual repair …)`). `doctor` clean before and after; the Phase 1 reconciliation copies the `payload` and the `a2.md` record body to `#002-outline-feel`. RUNNER-SPEC §5.1 is unaffected: it compares the verifier's `integrated_sha` with the phase head; a human gate's record `sha` is evidence only.
- **Reversal.** `mv .evidence/human/1.9/accepted.json.a1-backup .evidence/human/1.9/accepted.json`, delete `a2.md`, set `attempts 1`, `accepted_gate_attempt 1`, `finished_at 2026-09-08T07:18:15.982Z` under the lock, `git revert` this commit. `a1.md` was never touched.
- **Findings (Michael, 2026-09-08/09; backlog lines (a)–(c) appended, (d) is the pending plan).** (a) `doctor.mjs:102` suggests `scripts/gate.sh <id> --resume a<n>` for a non-CI human gate with a record on disk and no transition; `--resume` exists only in `ciGate`, `humanGate` ignores it, computes `n = last + 1` and calls `requireEligible`, so the line is a dead end where operators trust the output — the same family as this repair's cause: output that looks like a check or a fix and is not one. (b) `gate.sh` accepted a payload and a body that were the prepared command's own angle-bracket placeholders. (c) The 1.9 procedure checked the copied Markdown in one reader (Typora); Michael's record shows the same file rendering differently in BBEdit. A single-reader check is not a portability check; the next observation gate names two readers with different engines. (d) `ci.yml` has no Playwright step while `e2e/web/` holds the Phase 1 specs: they ran only inside the agents' Linux containers, never in CI, never on macOS or Windows; `1.verifyh` will fail on the missing `playwright-report`. The repair puts the failure in the job, not in the artifact check: the `1.verify.g1` task text makes the workflow run every spec file under `e2e/web/` on all three OSes and fail the job on any failing spec, so `conclusion !== success` and `ciGate` already refuses; the report is evidence for the reviewers, never the pass condition (#019). Handoff 009 carries the detail.
- **Not decided here.** Michael's `&#x20;` defect (any trailing space before a line break is escaped by the serializer; BBEdit lost the bullet lists on his file) is recorded in `a2.md` and escalated by him to a PRD §6 portability defect; the mechanism check and the BBEdit engine experiment are in handoff 009 Decisions; the reconciliation disposes of it with a fix task. H1 of #review-0-r2 was fixed at `ca1cd3d` on Michael's OK (its commit body is the record).

## #019-gate-failed-round-1.verifyh (2026-09-09, principal; recorded from Michael's stated decisions and rules, 1.9 idle window before 1.verify)

- **Decision: `1.verifyh` takes the designed `GATE-FAILED` round, not a pre-emptive planning commit.** Michael's reason, recorded: a pre-emptive fix means the gate never fails, so no plan request exists and the repair has no provenance record; RUNNER-SPEC §2 makes plan requests the only way a failed gate re-enters the graph, and this is the first live exercise of that path outside the conformance fixtures. One CI round on a public repo costs nothing, one failure is known, and what three real operating systems say at an integrated SHA is learned in the same round.
- **Rule for the fix task (the gate collects evidence, it does not grade it).** The `1.verify.g1` task text, written by the `plan.1.verifyh.r0` planning commit, puts the failure in the job: the workflow installs the Playwright browser, runs every spec file under `e2e/web/` against the Vite `webServer` on all three runners, and fails the job on any failing spec — so a failing suite is `conclusion !== success`, which `ciGate` already refuses. The `playwright-report` artifact is evidence for the 1.10 reviewers, never the pass condition; no artifact-content guard is built. Acceptance constraints, both required before the planning commit: (a) no literal spec count (CLAUDE.md forbids magnitudes copied from one dataset; the `[0.4]` lesson): the run executes every spec file under `e2e/web/`, the count comes from the directory, and the job fails on any failing spec; (b) the acceptance states plainly that a green `1.verify.g1h` proves the suite ran on all three runners at the integrated SHA, because that is the claim the phase review relies on. Backlog, not built: `ciGate` already records `{name, sha256, bytes}` per artifact in `run.json`/`accepted.json`, so an empty-report check needs no new evidence.
- **Rule: never amend a commit on `phase/N` once it has been pushed.** `4590b8d` was amended a minute after it was made; safe only because `origin/phase/1` was still at `ca1cd3d`. A push in between would have needed a force push, which `settings.json` denies. Added to the next-prompt constraints.
- **Rule: a command handed to Michael never contains example text to edit in place.** Either ask for the text first and hand the finished command, or leave a blank so obviously unfilled that running it as written fails (the 1.9 record was accepted because the gate validates presence, not sense — #repair-1.9 (b)). Added to the next-prompt constraints.
- **The BBEdit file (Michael, engine cmark), examined 2026-09-09.** `bbedit-no-bullet-points-example-file.md` (kept outside the tree: personal, untracked) holds 72 lines, no line starting with `-`, `*`, `+` or a number, and no `&#x20;` at any line end — the trailing spaces are literal (the two entities in it are the ones Michael typed inside quotes). BBEdit's bundled cmark renders it as paragraphs, correctly: there is no list syntax in the file to render. The lists and the entities were lost before BBEdit saw the file, on whatever path produced it (rendered-pane copy, or a save by another app that decoded the entities) — which path is not on record and is not to be invented. The `&#x20;` serializer behaviour stands as recorded in `a2.md` (mechanism confirmed in handoff 009 Decisions); the "bullet lists do not render in BBEdit" observation stays in the record as **not reproduced from the file**. Reversal: none (record only).

## #020-gate-1.verifyh-macos-keymap-and-playwright (2026-09-09, plan.1.verifyh.r0; principal, from the run's log; scope per DECISIONS #019 and handoff 010)

- **What happened.** `scripts/gate.sh 1.verifyh` a1 (ci.yml run 34333160626 at `3e98707`): `test (ubuntu-latest)` and `test (windows-latest)` green on every test file, `test (macos-latest)` failed at step `Test` — 2 test files, 5 tests: `packages/editor/test/store.test.ts` › `undoKeymap (rendered view)` › "undoes on Ctrl+z", "redoes on the shifted-letter route, where the event's key is 'Z'", "redoes on the keyCode route, where the event's key is not a letter", "is installed by storePlugins, ahead of the editing keymaps"; `packages/editor/test/toggle.test.ts` › `the chord` › "Cmd/Ctrl+/ reaches a ProseMirror keymap"; each `expected false to be true` on the handled boolean. `GATE-FAILED`, `1.verifyh` → `blocked`, plan request `plan.1.verifyh.r0`. `run.json` records `artifacts: []`: the `playwright-report` check (handoff 009 Finding 4, #019) was never reached, because artifacts are fetched only on `conclusion === success`.
- **Cause (principal reading; the fix task confirms it from the log and the library source before changing anything).** The bindings are `Mod-z`, `Mod-Z`, `Shift-Mod-z` (`packages/editor/src/store.ts` `KEY_NAMES`) and `Mod-/` (`packages/editor/src/toggle.ts` `TOGGLE_KEY`); PRD §6.5 says Cmd on macOS and Ctrl elsewhere, so the chord is right. `prosemirror-keymap` resolves `Mod-` from `navigator.platform` (`/Mac|iP(hone|[oa]d)/` → Meta, else Ctrl) in a module-level constant evaluated once at import; Node 22 exposes `navigator.platform` from the host OS (`MacIntel` on the macOS runner, `Linux x86_64` in the container). The five tests fire events with `ctrlKey: true` only: they encode the non-Mac route and pass on Linux and Windows by accident of platform. Code-caused (test code), not transient: no `gate.sh rerun`; any tree change is a `.g<n>` repair (RUNNER-SPEC §2).
- **Graph change (RUNNER-SPEC §2 rule (a)), two fix tasks, both physically in PRD §8 and `ralph/tasks.json`.** Appended after `1.verify`: **`1.11`** (opus; the two test files only; platform injected — stubbed before the library is imported, which needs module isolation because the constant is read at import — both routes, presence and absence, for each of the four chords; one test per guard, the journal naming the test that discharges each; no `.skip`/`runIf`; the CodeMirror name-level binding tests untouched; acceptance: reproduction with `navigator.platform` forced to `MacIntel` fails exactly the five, then green on both platforms, forcing stub not committed, both routes present by `grep -c` non-zero, honest statement that the only proof is the gate), **`1.12`** (sonnet; `.github/workflows/ci.yml` only: browser install per OS from the lockfile's version, `--with-deps` on Linux only; every spec file under `e2e/web/` run against the Vite `webServer` on all three runners; HTML report written on every outcome, never opened; a failing spec fails the step and the job — the failure lives in the job, `ciGate` refuses on `conclusion !== success`, no artifact-content guard (#019); `playwright-report-<os>` uploaded `if: always()` and merged into one `playwright-report` in `merge-logs`; acceptance (a) no literal spec count — `--list` compared as a set against `find`, (b) states that a green `1.verify.g1h` proves both suites ran on all three runners at the integrated SHA; the Windows job log named as the first thing to read on a failure), then **`1.verify.g1`** (`needsCI`, same `ci` object with `refTemplate ci/1.verify.g1/a{n}`, `artifactNames` unchanged, depends on `1.12`; `ciAcceptance` restates the claim and names `/logs/ci/1.verify.g1h/accepted/`, the gate id per #018). `1.10` now depends on `1.verify.g1`, so the generator rewires `1.10.r0a/b/c` to `1.verify.g1h`; `1.close` unchanged. `ralph/EXPECTED_COUNT` 150 → 154. At integration `sync-state` marks `1.verifyh` `superseded`. Next human gate: `scripts/gate.sh 1.verify.g1h`.
- **Model choice.** `1.11` is opus because the repair depends on seeing that the platform constant is evaluated at import (a stub after the import changes nothing) and on module isolation in vitest; `1.12` is sonnet because it is a workflow edit whose in-container proof is the command lines the 1.4/1.7/1.8 lessons already record.
- **For the 1.10 reviewers.** Whether the `1.11` tests cover every chord the product binds (four names across two files) or only the five that failed; whether the merged `playwright-report` holds three OS directories; the Windows Playwright log as the first unknown.
- **Reversal.** Before this planning commit integrates: `git -C .wt/plan.1.verifyh.r0 reset --hard phase/1` (the branch back to its cut at `3e98707`; the request stays `running`, the status `ralph.sh plan` left it in — `plan-retry` accepts only `blocked`/`integration-failed`, so no ralph command returns it to `pending`; the reversal is git-only, as in #018). After integration a plan request has no undo: `1.verifyh` stays `superseded` and any further change is another planning commit.

## #002-outline-feel (2026-09-09, task 1.10.r0d; the accepted record of human gate 1.9, attempt a2, copied by the Phase 1 reconciliation per RUNNER-SPEC §2 and §5.4; provenance: `a2.md` was transcribed by the principal from Michael's verbatim texts on 2026-09-09 under DECISIONS #repair-1.9, not written by `gate.sh`)

- **Record.** `.evidence/human/1.9/accepted.json`: `attempt 2`, `outcome ACCEPT`, `recorded_at 2026-09-09T05:59:10.836Z`, `evidence_path .evidence/human/1.9/a2.md`; `a2.md` header `sha ca1cd3d` (the head Michael observed). Gate text (PRD §8 1.9): Michael runs `pnpm dev`, writes ~1,000 words on /dev/editor, toggles source, copies Markdown and checks it in Typora; plays with /dev/outline; records his verdict and any re-scoping of Outline. The word count is not in the record (Grok finding 6, Sol's gate table): the gate validates presence, not sense (#repair-1.9 (b)); the next observation gate that names a size records the count in its payload.
- **`payload.verdictText` (verbatim):** The outline concept is worth building properly. The feel and the function both work, and producing a document from a topic plus a set of nested questions is the right shape for step 2. Three things to settle before it leaves prototype: whether the topic question is meant to disappear on Produce, which I suspect is deliberate and which I would want to be toggleable rather than fixed; what the generated headings are for, since if they stay as headings then the questions they came from serve no further purpose, and I could not tell whether I was supposed to delete them or keep them; and the muted question hint under each heading should be editable rather than read-only.
- **Record body (verbatim, `a2.md` `## Record`):**

> Rendered editor
>
> Double-clicking into a formatted line to edit it works well, and is a clear improvement over Typora, where editing a formatted line, especially a heading with a #, was the single most annoying thing about that app.
>
> Editing a bold span edits the characters inside the delimiters rather than the delimiters themselves. Tried with **. I expected to be able to change the formatting and instead got a character edit inside it.
>
> Ordered list items have too much vertical spacing between them. The background grey is too dark and should be lighter.
>
> Wanted: a word count toggle. Also an underline, though I understand Markdown has no underline syntax, so treat that as a question to decide rather than a feature to add.
>
> Source toggle
>
> The shortcut is Cmd+/ alone. I first read the instruction as Cmd+Shift+/ and was confused.
>
> I understand the two panes on this route are a dev harness rather than the product, so my judgment of the toggle itself is limited: with the raw Markdown always visible on the right I never needed Cmd+/ to see the source. Taking that into account, what I want in the real app is one pane in the centre, with an optional side-by-side raw view I can toggle on and off. Essentially VS Code in reverse. I also want a file tree on the left that toggles on and off, like Typora and VS Code.
>
> Defect: a trailing space before a line break is written as &#x20;
>
> Reproduction: type any character, then a space, then a line break. The line above gains &#x20; at the end in the Markdown.
>
> I first thought it happened after a period or comma. It also happens after ! and ?. Narrowing it down, it is any space before a line break, regardless of what precedes the space.
>
> This is not cosmetic. It breaks portability. In Typora the file renders correctly and the &#x20; disappears. In BBEdit's preview, my bullet lists do not render as lists at all. So the same file reads correctly in one third-party Markdown tool and incorrectly in another, which is the thing PRD §6 exists to prevent. The Typora check alone would have passed this.

- **What this reconciliation does with it.** The Outline verdict and the three things to settle (topic question disappearing on Produce → toggleable; what the generated headings are for once their questions are gone; the muted question hint editable) are inputs to the Phase 3 planning of the Outline mode (PRD §8 Phase 3), recorded here and not turned into Phase 1 tasks: the prototype met its gate. The editor and layout wishes (list spacing, background grey, word-count toggle, an underline decision, one centre pane with a toggleable side-by-side raw view and a toggleable file tree) are inputs to the Phase 2 shell tasks (2.1 onward); the `Cmd+/` vs `Cmd+Shift+/` misreading is a help-text note for the same place. The defect — any space before a line break written as `&#x20;` — is disposed of as **#review-1-r0 F1** (blocker, fix task 1.13): a PRD §6 portability defect, as Michael escalated it, with the mechanism from handoff 009 and the BBEdit file examination from #019 carried into that entry.

## #review-1-r0 (2026-09-09, task 1.10.r0d; principal reconciliation of review attempt r0 at implementation_sha e9ed88bc0b948c637af84ae559d50d09fbf090bb, verifier 1.verify.g1; Michael's decisions on F7, F8 and the reviewer record given in this session and recorded verbatim in substance)

verdict: FAIL

- **Reviewer outcomes** (`.evidence/reviews/1/r0/<reviewer>/status.json`). Claude (Opus, cold build in `/scratch/claude`, four Playwright drivers and three vitest probes, one mutation, 1,524/1,524, 83/83 runner tests): FAIL, 1 blocker / 6 should-fix / 5 nits. Sol (Codex, cold build, browser probes saved under its `/report/`, 36/36 e2e locally): FAIL, 0 / 6 / 1, every should-fix marked Required. Grok (read-only, cites `/logs/ci/1.verify.g1h/accepted/`, secret-pattern walk of the tree): PASS, 0 / 2 / 4. Reports byte-identical in `docs/reviews/phase-1-r0-{claude,sol,grok}.md`. All three verified both accepted artifact digests independently (`test-logs` `e1148a23…` 104,674 bytes / 10 files; `playwright-report` `dd90f3d8…` 1,681,049 bytes / 3 files; MATCH), `verification_sha == implementation_sha == e9ed88b`, `verifier_id 1.verify.g1`, run 34338761650, three OS directories in each artifact, `36 passed` Playwright on ubuntu, macos and windows, 1,524 vitest on each. The 1.9 record (`recordTarget 002-outline-feel`) is copied above as **#002-outline-feel**.
- **Verdict per PRD §7 criterion.** Dev route with the load-fixture / copy-Markdown bar: pass (3/3). Michael writes ~1,000 words in the rendered view and toggles to source: pass on the record (3/3; the count is not in the record — F16). The copied Markdown round-trips: **fail** — it round-trips in the formal sense (invariants A/B/C green, Typora renders it) but the string the copy button produces is not the Markdown that was typed (F1), and the editor's own copy/paste discards structure (F2). Outline verdict recorded: pass (3/3; copied above). Full suite and every e2e/web spec green on three OSes at the implementation SHA, digests matched: pass (3/3). Zero dependencies beyond PRD §4: fail (F7). Phase check, typing latency p95 with 10k words on /dev/editor: 14–15 ms (Claude, keydown → DOM mutation), 38.6/39.5 ms (Sol, beforeinput → next timer task), unverifiable (Grok) — the PRD sentence's alternative, "a follow-up task is inserted at reconciliation", is taken (F5). One blocker and six required should-fixes → **FAIL** (RUNNER-SPEC §5.4). `cargo test` is still vacuous (0/0/0 on three OSes; the Rust surface is Phase 2), stated again so the Phase 2 gate is read as the first real Rust evidence.
- **Findings, severity as reconciled, disposition** (fix tasks appended in this commit, physically in PRD §8 and `ralph/tasks.json`; F-numbers are used by the task texts):
  - F1 **blocker** (Claude 1, Sol 6 Required, Grok 1; Michael's 1.9 record, escalated by him): a paragraph or heading edited in the rendered view keeps ProseMirror's text verbatim, so an ordinary trailing space before Enter reaches the serializer, whose `unsafe` rule escapes a space before a line ending as `&#x20;`; `parse` already strips that space, so the editor builds a tree no Markdown file parses to and the copied Markdown is not a `format(parse(x))` fixed point. Reproduced end to end by two reviewers and by Michael. **Disposed as a PRD §6 portability defect**: §6's promise is that the file reads the same in any Markdown reader, and a trailing space at a paragraph end is not content — CommonMark strips it, micromark strips it, so the editor must not hand it to the serializer; the serializer's escape is correct and stays (Grok). The mechanism was confirmed on the tree in handoff 009 (Decisions) and the BBEdit file was examined in #019: that file holds literal trailing spaces and no list syntax, so "bullet lists do not render in BBEdit" stays **not reproduced from the file** — Sol's caution (entity emission proven, the BBEdit cause not) is recorded and changes nothing, because the fix is required on the mechanism alone. → **1.13** (opus): strip at the block boundary in `pmToMdast`, ownership rule in the doc comment, break-adjacent/inline-code/raw untouched, one test per guard, and the corpus leg Claude's risk 1 asks for — the `editor fixed point` leg seeded from a typing-shaped transaction over every fixture — plus the Playwright presence and absence cases. No test in 1,524 could see this because every round-trip leg is seeded from `parse(fixture)` (lesson, and the rule promoted via 1.verify.r1).
  - F2 **should-fix, required** (Sol 1 Required): the schema has `toDOM` for everything and no `parseDOM`, so the editor's native copy → paste turns `# Heading` + `**bold**` into plain text. → **1.14** (opus): `parseDOM` paired one-to-one with each `toDOM`, the raw atom round-tripping by its stored value and never re-parsed as markup, an enumeration test over `schema.nodes`/`marks`, a Playwright self copy/paste leg over every index.json fixture.
  - F3 **should-fix, required** (Sol 4 Required): the position map derives table row/cell ranges from descendants, so an empty cell or empty row — the shape the 1.3 input rule creates — has no range. → **1.15** (opus): explicit ranges from the table layout, zero-width ownership rule stated, first/middle/last empty cells, an LF fixture with empty cells, the toggle from the new empty row.

- **Findings, severity as reconciled, disposition** (fix tasks appended in this commit, physically in PRD §8 and `ralph/tasks.json`; G-numbers are used by the task texts, the way #review-0-r1 used G after F):
  - G1 **blocker** (Claude 1 blocker, Sol 1 Required, Grok 3 nit): "Copy Markdown" (`apps/desktop/src/dev/DevEditor.tsx:206`) formats the store's last committed root without flushing the source binding, so during a source burst — which since 1.17 is committed one coalescing window after the last keystroke — it copies stale text and reports success; the markdown pane lags for the same window. Reproduced in a browser by Claude and Sol; Grok read it from the tree. Rated blocker because it is a silent wrong-output path in the one control PRD §7's gate criterion names, introduced by this phase's own fix chain, with zero coverage; a regression of a passed task (1.17) is a new task, never an edit of 1.17. → **1.23** (sonnet): the flush as the first statement of `copyMarkdown`, the Playwright case inside the window, and — Claude's lesson 2, which is the class — every reader of the store enumerated from the code (`grep getState()`) with its flush status, so the next reader starts from a list rather than from a task text.
  - G2 **should-fix, required** (Sol 2 Required; the sibling of G1 in the same seam): the source view's Undo/Redo run on the committed history while the burst being typed is pending, and the binding's `pull` drops the pending text when the undone root arrives (`store.ts:258`, `toggle.ts:609`): an immediate Undo on a fresh route does nothing; after a completed burst, Undo removes that burst and Redo restores it without the pending text. Reading the mechanism: the deferred commit stamps `at: now()` at the moment it fires, so a flush shortly after the previous commit coalesces a burst the user typed 1.2 s later into the previous entry — which is why Sol's second reproduction removed `first` as well — and a flush followed by more typing opens a second entry for one burst (1.17's own lesson). → **1.24** (opus): history commands settle the pending burst first, from one seam; the commit's `at` is the keystroke time recorded in `change`; the grouping rule stated once; one injected-clock test per case; the two reproductions as real chords.

Remedy scope, decided after reading the three bodies: the whole document, not the edited paragraph — the constant's own doc comment says rewriting the user's line breaks is not a trade worth making, and the formatter already preserves soft breaks; Sol's remedy (node whitespace settings on the framework path that reparses) is taken, Grok's (a separate inline node for the soft break) is not — mdast writes a soft break as `\n` inside a text node and the schema maps 1:1 to mdast (PRD §4). → **1.25** (opus), together with G4.
