- **The reviewer record, counted from the three reports (Michael's rule, #review-1-r0; post.1's input; Michael's reading of the numbers, this session).** Findings 9 / 4 / 4 (Claude / Sol / Grok). Unique findings (raised by one reviewer only): **Claude 6 of 9** (2, 4, 6, 7, 8, 9), **Sol 2 of 4** (2, 4), **Grok 1 of 4** (4). Shared findings, three: G1 — Claude blocker, Sol should-fix Required, Grok nit; G3 — should-fix ×3, unanimous; G5 — Grok should-fix, Claude nit. The cumulative table over the fourteen shared findings of the project so far (a reviewer is "low" when it rated a shared finding below the highest rating on it; recounted from the reports for this entry, which corrects the count the principal first gave Michael in this session — Sol's should-fix on G1 is below Claude's blocker, so Sol is 2, not 1): Phase 0 F1 (blocker ×3), F3 (should-fix ×3), F4 (should-fix ×3), F8 (Claude low), F12 (Grok low), G6 (Grok low); Phase 1 r0 F1 (Sol low, Grok low), F7 (should-fix ×2), F11 (nit ×2), F9 colour probe (nit ×3), F13 (nit ×2); r1 G1 (Sol low, Grok low), G3 (should-fix ×3), G5 (Claude low). Disagreements 6 of 14; low-rater instances **Grok 4, Claude 2, Sol 2**. **Michael's reading, recorded:** Grok is the most lenient, but n is six disagreements and Claude was low twice, so this is a signal, not a verdict. The sharper number is the blocker itself: one defect (Copy Markdown stale) drew blocker / should-fix / nit from the three reviewers, and a three-way spread on one defect says the severity vocabulary is not calibrated between reviewers — a template problem, cheap to fix. **Decisions:** one line added to `docs/reviews/TEMPLATE.md` in this commit defining what separates blocker from should-fix from nit, with an example of each (the r2 reviewers read the template at implementation_sha, which includes this commit); Grok's inputs are not touched; no reviewer change inside this review set (#review-1-r0 rule); the `1.10.r2c` text asks Grok to state why its G1 severity differed; post.1 gets this table; the backlog's reviewer-prompt option keeps its condition.
- **Michael's decisions this session.** (1) **Turn budget: the r2 chain runs at 50 as five single-concern tasks.** The count, read from `.evidence/state/tasks.json` and `runner.log`: 10 of the 23 Phase 1 loop tasks needed more than one attempt — 1.3, 1.4, 1.5, 1.7, 1.8, 1.14, 1.15, 1.16, 1.17 at 2 and 1.21 at 3 — and nine of the ten first attempts ended `NO-JOURNAL` (1.4's ended exit 1, as did 1.21's second). `NO-JOURNAL` calls `stuckOr` (`ralph/lib/run.mjs:127`): the attempt counts toward the three, and the runner stops with a signal that needs a manual restart — so those ten tasks each spent one of three attempts on paperwork, and the principal restarted the runner nine times for it. The knob is `ralph/lib/run.mjs:108` (`maxTurns = SETUP_TASKS ? 30 : 50`), **not** `docker/compose.yml`, whose `RALPH_MAX_TURNS` default is a fallback the runner always overrides (handoff 013 named the wrong file; no image rebuild is involved). **The Phase 2 raise is conditional, not approved.** Evidence read from three of the nine capped transcripts (`/logs/tasks/{1.14,1.17,1.21}/1.log`) plus the other six, by the transcript's own measure (tool-use blocks and the result line's `num_turns`): every capped first attempt ended `error_max_turns` at `num_turns` 51 with 54–67 tool uses and zero `git commit` calls, in the verification tail — 1.14's last twelve tool uses were lint, the suites, the e2e run, the diff-scope check and two reads of the journal template; 1.17's were the before/after measurement, e2e, lint, the unit suite and coverage; 1.21's were seventeen suite-shaped runs — and the runner's recovery commit checkpointed the dirty tree, so attempt 2 started from the finished implementation and took `num_turns` 32–48. The attempts that finished at the first try in the same chain measure the same way: 1.13 at 52 tool uses / `num_turns` 53, 1.18 at 56 / 57. Success and failure sit within a few turns of each other at the cap, so the transcript measure is unimodal around 50–60 and the cap sits on the median of an opus review-fix task's real work; the journal's self-reported "Tool calls" (27 for 1.13, ~24 for 1.18, and the 10–31 range Michael read) undercount the transcript by about 2×, which is why the counts looked bimodal — the `docs/progress.md` metrics column carries that caveat from now on. **Proposal, with that evidence: 80 turns for `loop` tasks** (1.6× the cap; every capped attempt was within the verification tail, whose remaining work the cold attempt 2 re-did at 32–48 turns because it re-oriented from nothing), setup 30 and reviewers 160 unchanged, as one `fix(runner)` commit with its conformance leg in the Phase 2 boundary window — taken only on Michael's OK after the r2 chain has run at 50 under the journal-stub rule below, and read by the transcript measure, never by the journal's self-report. **Taken now, in the r2 chain (Michael):** the journal entry moves earlier in CLAUDE.md's iteration order — a stub before implementing, completed at the end — so a capped attempt leaves a checkpoint the runner reads as a journal entry instead of nothing; it rides through `promote-lessons` in `1.verify.r2` as verified code, needs no runner change, and targets the actual `NO-JOURNAL` failure: with the stub present a capped attempt is recorded as "no DONE promise" (`run.mjs:147`, no stop signal, the loop continues into the next attempt from the checkpoint) rather than `NO-JOURNAL` (a stop signal and a restart). It still counts toward the three attempts (§4.3), and the entry says so. RUNNER-SPEC §10 gains the sentence that a task's own stub is completed in place before integration and the integrated commit appends exactly one line per attempt. (2) **1.17's 25× factor stays; no code task. The reasoning, recorded and not only the decision:** the measurement spread is 20–25 ms on the CI runners against 9.5–11.7 ms on a quiet machine, about 2× from the environment alone, and tightening 25× to 10× is a 2.5× move, so it would set a threshold inside its own noise; no reviewer asked to tighten it, and the factor's job is a superlinear keystroke path (a ratio near 100), which it still catches. Taken instead: G10's trigger wording (the quiet single-worker number is the input), G9's commit metric on the backlog, and the r2 reviewer text asking for the isolated run separately from any contended or CI number; the incremental `pmToMdast` backlog line keeps both its triggers, with Phase 2's autosave named as the first consumer to watch.
- **Runner and gate observations this block (no deviation, nothing added).** Handoffs 012 and 013 were cherry-picked into this worktree (deviation 29 procedure, fourth use); both rotations rewrote `docs/handoffs/next-prompt.md` from the same base, so the second cherry-pick conflicted on that file only and was resolved to the 013 version (the newer), byte-identical to `git show handoff/013:docs/handoffs/next-prompt.md`. The gate and the review set both ran clean at attempt 1, nothing was retried, re-cut or repaired. `origin` holds `ci/1.verify/a1` (stale) and `ci/1.verify.r1/a1` (accepted): `gate.sh gc` by Michael at the phase boundary.
- **Graph change (RUNNER-SPEC §5.4, §3).** Also in this commit: one sentence in `docs/reviews/TEMPLATE.md` (severity definitions, above) and one in RUNNER-SPEC §10 (the journal stub). Appended after `1.10.r1d` in PRD §8, a linear chain: `1.23` → `1.24` → `1.25` → `1.26` → `1.27` → `1.verify.r2` (`needsCI`, `ci/1.verify.r2/a{n}`, artifacts `test-logs` and `playwright-report`, its generated gate `1.verify.r2h`) → `1.10.r2a/b/c` (depend on `1.verify.r2`, rewritten to `1.verify.r2h` by the generator) → `1.10.r2d` (this role again, a fresh session); `1.close` now depends on `1.10.r2d`. `1.23` depends on `1.10.r1d` so the chain starts when this commit integrates. `ralph/EXPECTED_COUNT` 170 → 181. Also in this commit: the two PRD §4 sentences (G12). Every fix task's text carries the rule that a defect found outside its scope gets a backlog line with a revisit trigger and a hard stop in the same commit — the lesson three reports wrote independently, made a task obligation rather than a hope. Five fix tasks is the honest size of the review: one blocker, five required should-fixes in three code areas (the deferred-commit seam, the whitespace family, two test gaps), every task one iteration with its file list named.
- **Promote-lessons for `1.verify.r2` (RUNNER-SPEC §5.5): one rule and one procedure change**, both carried verbatim in that task's text. The rule — every reader of the document store that is not an editing surface settles the pending source burst before it reads, and a new reader's test is an action inside the coalescing window; discharged by 1.23's copy-inside-the-window case and 1.24's undo-inside-the-window cases. The procedure change (Michael, above) — `## Every iteration` steps 2 and 3 rewritten so the journal entry is stubbed and committed before implementing and completed at the end; discharged by `ralph/check-agent-rules.sh` and `tests/agent-rules.test.ts` (the two files identical below line 1) and, for its purpose, by the first capped attempt in the r2 chain that the runner records as "no DONE promise" rather than `NO-JOURNAL`.
- **Backlog** lines appended to `docs/V1.1-BACKLOG.md`: G7 (coverage include, hard stop `2.verify`), G9 (commit metric), G10 (the trigger's measurement named), G11 (the bold-span line), the reviewer-prompt trigger refinement, the conditional turn-budget raise at the Phase 2 boundary with its evidence, the emphasis-edge member of the `&#x20;` family for Phase 3 planning, and Michael's stated-invariant sweep.
- **Michael's note on F7 / 1.19 (this session, recorded here because #review-1-r0 is a passed task's record and is not edited).** Confirmed on his side: `codemirror` is gone from `packages/editor/package.json`, no `codemirror@` block and no `@codemirror/commands` anywhere in `pnpm-lock.yaml`, and `source.ts` now explains the history rather than asserting the property. The `no-codemirror-history` test is the right call and better than what he asked for: it turned a comment somebody has to remember into something that fails a build. His generalisation, which is the lesson of this phase: it applies to every other "we deliberately do not do X" comment in the tree, and a sweep of which stated invariants have no test behind them is worth doing at some point — backlog line, revisit at Phase 2 planning, so the sweep is a test-only task and not a review finding.
- **Reversal.** Before this commit integrates: `git -C .wt/1.10.r1d reset --hard phase/1` (the task stays `principal-pending`, the status `run` left it in; the cherry-picked handoffs return to `handoff/012` and `handoff/013`, which are not deleted until after `1.close`). After integration a reconciliation has no undo: its verdict is written to `.evidence/reviews/1/r1/verdict`, the r2 chain is in the graph, and any change is a further planning commit.

## #021-gate-1.verify.r2h-windows-clipboard-crlf (2026-09-10, plan.1.verify.r2h.r0; principal, from the run's log; scope per DECISIONS #019 and handoff 014)

- **What happened.** `scripts/gate.sh 1.verify.r2h` a1 (ci.yml run 34499653269 at `6d9345c`): `test (ubuntu-latest)` and `test (macos-latest)` green on every step, unit suite and every e2e/web spec included; `test (windows-latest)` green through `Cargo test` and failed at step `Playwright (e2e/web)`: 3 failed, 65 passed, all three in `e2e/web/editor-clipboard.spec.ts` › `Copy Markdown flushes a pending source burst before it reads the store` (the presence, absence and rendered-view cases task 1.23 added for G1), each `expect(clipboard).toBe("<word>\n")` receiving the word plus an invisible trailing character before the newline. `GATE-FAILED`, `1.verify.r2h` → `blocked`, plan request `plan.1.verify.r2h.r0`. `run.json` records `artifacts: []` (artifacts are fetched only on `conclusion === success`, #020), so the three-OS-directories check was not reached; the gate's own log (`.evidence/ci/1.verify.r2h/a1/workflow.log`) is the evidence. The `merge-logs` job did not run.
- **Cause (principal reading; the fix task confirms it from the log before changing anything).** The three tests read the OS clipboard back through `navigator.clipboard.readText()` after `copyMarkdown` (`apps/desktop/src/dev/DevEditor.tsx`) wrote it with `navigator.clipboard.writeText(text)`. On Windows, Chromium hands plain text to the system clipboard with LF converted to CRLF, so the read returns the word, a carriage return and a newline where the assertion expects the newline alone. The app wrote the right bytes (`format(root)` ends in `\n`, the same string the Linux and macOS legs read back); the assertion encoded one platform's clipboard convention — the same class as #018 (CRLF at checkout) and #020 (a `Mod-` chord resolved from the host platform): green in the container and on two runners by accident of platform. The spec's own doc comment (task 1.14) already states why a copy case should not go through the OS clipboard: it is one shared resource across parallel workers on three OSes; 1.23's three cases are the only copy cases in the tree that do. Code-caused (test code), not transient: no `gate.sh rerun`; any tree change is a `.g<n>` repair (RUNNER-SPEC §2).
- **Scope decision (explicit, so the fix task does not pick).** Three candidates: (1) normalise the carriage return away in the assertion; (2) branch the expected value on `process.platform`; (3) spy `navigator.clipboard.writeText` before the page's scripts run (`page.addInitScript`) and assert the string the app handed the clipboard API, byte-exact. Chosen: (3). It is strictly better, not a compromise: it asserts the app's own output on every OS with no normalisation (1 would hide a stray carriage return the app might one day write), encodes no platform list (2, forbidden by the [plan.1.verifyh.r0] lesson and the next-prompt rule), keeps the real code path under test (flush → format → writeText → the `"Copied Markdown"` status), removes the shared-OS-clipboard hazard the spec's doc comment names, and drops the `clipboard-read` permission grant those three tests no longer need. The foreign-paste case keeps the real clipboard: pasting content from outside the editor is exactly the case where the source has to be outside the editor. G1's guard stays live: the task re-runs 1.23's flush-removal mutation against the spy and records that the presence case alone turns red.
- **Graph change (RUNNER-SPEC §2 rule (a)), both physically in PRD §8 and `ralph/tasks.json`.** Appended after `1.verify.r2`: **`1.28`** (opus; `e2e/web/editor-clipboard.spec.ts` only; the spy, the three grep guards — no `readText`, no `process.platform`, no carriage-return escape in code — the mutation, the byte-exact assertion; honest statement that the only proof is the gate) and **`1.verify.r2.g1`** (sonnet; `needsCI`, same `ci` object with `refTemplate ci/1.verify.r2.g1/a{n}`, `artifactNames` unchanged, depends on `1.28`; the promote-lessons and CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md steps of `1.verify.r2` are already integrated at `6d9345c` and are not repeated; `ciAcceptance` restates the three-OS claim and names `/logs/ci/1.verify.r2.g1h/accepted/`). The r2 reviewers are explicit physical tasks (#review-1-r1), so the rewire is in the PRD rows themselves: `1.10.r2a/b/c` now depend on `1.verify.r2.g1` (the generator's `needsCI` expansion makes that `1.verify.r2.g1h`), and the four `/logs/ci/1.verify.r2h/accepted/` references in their descriptions (Claude 1, Sol 1, Grok 2) read `/logs/ci/1.verify.r2.g1h/accepted/`, because that is where the accepted artifacts will be; `1.10.r2d` and `1.close` unchanged. `ralph/EXPECTED_COUNT` 181 → 184 (`generate-tasks: 141 raw + 21 review-set + 22 CI gates = 184 expanded`). At integration `sync-state` marks `1.verify.r2h` `superseded`. Next human gate: `scripts/gate.sh 1.verify.r2.g1h`.
- **Model choice.** `1.28` is opus although the diff is small: the container cannot observe the failing platform, so a miss costs a full CI round plus another planning commit, and the task has to keep the G1 guard live (the mutation against the spy) while removing the OS clipboard from the read path — one wrong step (a spy installed after navigation, a spy that does not resolve, a normalisation slipped into the assertion) is invisible in the container. `1.verify.r2.g1` is sonnet, as every verifier so far.
- **For the 1.10 reviewers.** Whether any other spec still reads the OS clipboard for a copy assertion (the tree's `readText` count after 1.28); whether the spy leg still fails when the flush is removed (the journal names the mutation); whether the merged `playwright-report` holds three OS directories; the Windows Playwright log stays the first unknown of every gate.
- **Reversal.** Before this planning commit integrates: `git -C .wt/plan.1.verify.r2h.r0 reset --hard phase/1` (the branch back to its cut at `6d9345c`); the request stays `running`, the status `ralph.sh plan` left it in — `plan-retry` accepts only `blocked`/`integration-failed` and `plan-abandon` moves it to `abandoned`, so no ralph command returns it to `pending`; the reversal is git-only, as in #018 and #020. After integration a plan request has no undo: `1.verify.r2h` stays `superseded` and any further change is another planning commit.

## #review-1-r2 (2026-09-11, task 1.10.r2d; principal reconciliation of review attempt r2 at implementation_sha c9408ee0e0de5733fb3b14ce96d83904e03ff26f, verifier 1.verify.r2.g1, gate 1.verify.r2.g1h ACCEPT a1, run 34509206442; an autonomous session — no decision in this entry is Michael's, and every conditional item stays conditional on his OK)

verdict: FAIL

- **Reviewer outcomes** (`.evidence/reviews/1/r2/<reviewer>/status.json`; reports byte-identical in `docs/reviews/phase-1-r2-{claude,sol,grok}.md`). Claude (Opus, cold build in `/scratch/claude`, five throwaway Playwright probe specs and three vitest probes, six mutations reverted, 2,043/2,043, 68/68 Playwright, 83/83 runner tests): **FAIL**, 1 blocker / 3 should-fix / 2 nits, 6 findings. Sol (Codex, cold build in `/scratch/sol`, independent browser drivers `probe.mjs` and `control.mjs` kept under its `/report/` with their logs, isolated `latency.spec.ts --workers=1`, artifact hashing, credential scan): **FAIL**, 1 / 0 / 1, 2 findings. Grok (read-only, cites `/logs/ci/1.verify.r2.g1h/accepted/`, `digestDir` recomputed in Python, secret-pattern walk): **PASS**, 0 / 0 / 2, 2 findings. All three verified both accepted artifact digests (`test-logs` `143475993e…` 108,838 bytes / 10 files; `playwright-report` `378a74cbb1…` 1,915,157 bytes / 3 files; MATCH), `verification_sha == implementation_sha == c9408ee`, `verifier_id 1.verify.r2.g1`, three OS directories in each artifact, 2,043 vitest and 68 Playwright (13 spec files, counted from the directory) per OS. No human gate ran in this attempt, so no record is copied here (#002-outline-feel already holds the 1.9 record).
- **Verdict per PRD §7 criterion.** Dev route with the bar: pass (3/3). Michael writes ~1,000 words and toggles to source: pass on the record (Claude, Grok) / unverifiable for the word count (Sol); F16 stands, unchanged. **The copied Markdown round-trips: fail** — Claude reproduced in Chromium at this SHA a paragraph ending in a `hard_break` (H1: seed `hard-break.md`'s shape, `Ctrl+End`, three `Backspace`), which `format` writes as a bare trailing `\` that `parse` reads back as a literal backslash while the status reads "Copied Markdown"; Sol's "pass for the exercised round-trip cases" and Grok's pass from the tree do not contradict it — neither deleted. Outline verdict recorded: pass (3/3). Full suite and every e2e/web spec green on three OSes at the implementation SHA, digests matched: pass (3/3). Zero dependencies beyond PRD §4: pass (3/3; `check-deps` 61 npm + 4 cargo allowlisted in both cold builds and on all three runners). **§6.5, store-owned undo preserves the continuous typing group: fail** — Sol's `/report/probe.mjs`: `a`, Copy Markdown, then `bcdef` at 300 ms per character, Undo at once leaves `a` (H2), where the same burst without the Copy undoes to empty (`control.mjs`). Phase check, typing latency p95 with 10k words under the 1.17 protocol: **pass on a quiet machine** — rendered pane p95 9.2 ms (Claude, single worker, n=120) and 9.8 ms (Sol, isolated rerun); 20.6 / 22.7 / 11.9 ms on the ubuntu / windows / macos runners of the accepted gate, reported separately as G10 asked; the 25× decision (#review-1-r1) stands. Two blockers, each unique to one reviewer, each with a browser reproduction → **FAIL** (RUNNER-SPEC §5.4). `cargo test` is still vacuous (0/0/0 on three OSes; the Rust surface is Phase 2).
- **Findings, severity as reconciled, disposition** (fix tasks appended in this commit, physically in PRD §8 and `ralph/tasks.json`; H-numbers are used by the task texts, the way #review-1-r1 used G after F; "1.13/1.25 territory" means the defect lives in a passed task's file — the fix is a new task, never an edit of the passed record, DECISIONS #016):
  - H1 **blocker** (Claude 1): `stripUnparsableWhitespace` (`packages/editor/src/schema.ts:674`; its ownership rule at `:640-670`) leaves a `hard_break` that is a block's **last** node in place; `format` writes it as a bare trailing `\`, and `parse` reads that back as a literal backslash (`format(parse("one\\\n"))` is `"one\\\\\n"`, the `break` node gone), so Copy Markdown puts non-round-tripping bytes on the clipboard and reports success. Reachable in three `Backspace` presses from `hard-break.md`'s shape. The r0 F1 / r1 G4 family: a tree the editor can build that `parse` cannot produce; 1.25's eleven boundary guards cover whitespace at every boundary and never an atom at a block end, and both corpus legs only ever `insertText` (`packages/editor/test/typing-legs.ts`), so a tree that needs a deletion to build is outside every guard. → **1.29** (opus): the ownership rule extended to atoms at a block end — a trailing `hard_break` is dropped the way a run trimmed to nothing is dropped, repeatedly until the last node is neither (CommonMark §6.7: a backslash at a paragraph's end is a literal backslash, not a break) — with one guard per position (a break as the block's last node, a block that is only a break, a break followed only by whitespace, on `paragraph` and on `heading`) asserting `format` bytes and the parse∘format fixed point, one browser case that deletes rather than types, and — Claude's lesson 2, the class — a corpus leg seeded from **deletion-shaped** transactions (`tr.delete`), not only `insertText`, over every fixture.
  - H2 **blocker** (Sol 1): a flush inside a continuous source typing burst still splits undo history when the continuation outlasts one coalescing window (`packages/editor/src/toggle.ts:628`): `pendingAt` keeps only the pending segment's *latest* keystroke time, so `push`'s one distance (`packages/core/src/undo.ts`, `at - present.at`) compares the previous entry's last keystroke with the segment's last keystroke and never sees the adjacent ones in between — 1.24's grouping rule ("the chain of keystrokes that becomes one commit is the chain that became one coalesced entry before 1.17") holds for a burst shorter than a window and fails for a longer one, and 1.24's case 3 stops its continuation at 200 ms. Sol's times: 242, 308, 612, 924, 1237, 1543 ms — every adjacent gap under 314 ms, the two commits 1,300 ms apart. → **1.31** (opus): the pending segment carries its first and its last keystroke time; continuity is decided between the segment's *first* keystroke and the previous entry's *last* (`PushOptions` gains a `from`, defaulting to `at`; the entry keeps `at` as the latest), explicit closure on toggle and history actions preserved; one injected-clock test per case (a continuation longer than the window with every adjacent gap shorter, timed and flushed endings; the genuine-gap absence case; the rendered view unchanged) and Sol's reproduction as a real Playwright case. 1.24/1.26 territory. G9's revisit trigger fires in this task (it changes `bindCodeMirror`'s commit path after 1.24): the latency spec's third reported line (`commit: p50/p95, n=<bursts>`), evidence only.
  - H3 **should-fix, required** (Claude 2): the flush `toggle` performs before it reads the store (`apps/desktop/src/dev/DevEditor.tsx:102`) has no test that fails without it — 68/68 stay green with the line removed — and Claude's probes found no behavioural difference either, which points at the line being redundant with `SourceBinding.destroy`'s own flush on the mode-change effect cleanup while its comment asserts an ordering it is no longer the cause of. `toggle` is the first reader the rule promoted via `1.verify.r2` names, and the only one of the four without a coalescing-window case. → **1.32** (opus): the toggle-inside-the-window case (type in source, toggle at once, the rendered document holds the burst), the mutation run against **both** candidate guards (`:102` and `destroy`'s `commitPending()`), and the line kept only if a case fails without it — otherwise the comment rewritten to name `destroy` as the guard and the line as the reader's own statement of the rule; the journal names the test that discharges the toggle clause.
  - H4 **should-fix, required** (Claude 3; a direct consequence of #021's scope decision, recorded as such): after 1.28, `installWriteTextSpy` is the first statement of `openEditor`, the only route to `clickCopyMarkdown`, so every click of the gate criterion's own control goes to a stub that always resolves, and no test in the tree exercises the real clipboard write; in the container the unspied click fails with `Copy failed: the browser refused clipboard access`, so the `catch` branch is reachable in CI's own environment and covered by nothing. #021's choice (the spy, byte-exact) was right for the assertion and silently removed the last end-to-end evidence that the button works — Claude's lesson 3. → **1.32**: one case that grants `clipboard-read`/`clipboard-write`, clicks Copy Markdown with no spy installed, and asserts only that the status becomes "Copied Markdown" and that `readText()` **contains** the copied word — platform-independent (a carriage return does not break "contains"), still red if `writeText` rejects; the three spy cases untouched; the spec's doc comment states both rules.
  - H5 **nit, taken in 1.29** (Claude 5): a `heading` whose last inline node is a `hard_break` serialises to `"H\\\n\n"` and reparses as a *paragraph* — H1's root cause one level up, block type lost, proved headlessly. Its own guard on `heading`, so the structure loss is pinned separately.
  - H6 **nit, taken in 1.29** (Claude 6): a `table_cell` holding a line ending serialises as `| a&#xA;b |` — a fixed point, so no invariant is broken, but a numeric character reference in the user's file, and `whitespace: INLINE_PRE` on `table_cell` (1.25) is what lets a `\n` reach a cell at all. A GFM cell cannot hold a line ending (the row would end), so the ownership rule gains the cell's own clause: a line-ending run inside a `table_cell` collapses to one space, guarded at the unit level and probed in the browser through a multi-line plain-text paste into a cell (the one route the typing keymap does not consume); if unreachable, the guard still stands and the journal says so.
  - H7 **should-fix, taken as a runner chore at the phase boundary — not a fix task** (Claude 4 should-fix, Grok 2 nit; the one shared finding): `ralph/PROMPT.md` lines 25–34, the per-task wrapper's "Iteration" list, still says implement at step 2, one post-hoc journal entry at step 4, one commit at step 6, while `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` at the same SHA carry the six-step stub-first list `1.verify.r2` installed (RUNNER-SPEC §10). It cost one attempt: `1.verify.r2.g1` a1 stopped at orientation on the contradiction (`journal-main.md` 2026-09-10T16:53:30Z, `Status: Blocked`); a2 applied CLAUDE.md's stated precedence and passed. The wrapper is rendered by the runner from the host checkout (`docker/entrypoints/claude-task:20` reads `/work/ralph/PROMPT.md`), it is a runner file, and a runner file that contradicts RUNNER-SPEC is fixed by one atomic `fix(runner)` commit with its conformance test (Michael's standing instruction), which never lands on `phase/1` mid-flight and cannot ride this commit (§8.1 allowlist). Disposition: **(a)** at the Phase 1→2 boundary, from the host checkout with the runner idle (rotate.md step 5, first case): `fix(runner): ralph/PROMPT.md "Iteration" steps 2–6 replaced by CLAUDE.md's steps 2–5 verbatim`, and `tests/agent-rules.test.ts` widened so the wrapper's iteration list is asserted against CLAUDE.md's (the shape of the existing `CLAUDE.md ≡ AGENTS.md` check; Claude's fix), so the next procedure change cannot leave the wrapper behind; backlog line with the hard stop. **(b)** Until then, every r3-chain task text names the reading that stands — CLAUDE.md's stub-first list outranks the wrapper's stale step count by the wrapper's own line 7, and the mismatch is mechanical (commit granularity), not a substance conflict to stop on — which is `1.verify.r2.g1`'s second lessons line (2026-09-10T17:05:00Z), the one that stands over its first (2026-09-10T16:53:30Z, which recommended stopping); neither line is edited.
  - H8 **should-fix, required — the r1 backlog deferral reopened** (Sol 2, filed as a nit against the backlog's *claim*; rated here by the consequence, per the TEMPLATE rule): `docs/V1.1-BACKLOG.md:44` defers the `&#x20;` family's emphasis-edge member as "unreachable while Phase 1 binds no mark-toggle command", and that premise is false — load `a *b* c`, put the rendered caret after `b`, type a space: `a *b&#x20;*&#x20;c`, by ordinary keyboard events (`/report/probe.mjs`). Marks come from input rules and from every loaded file, and a caret at the end of an emphasised word sits inside the mark, so this is a one-keystroke route in the rendered view to the byte family Michael escalated at r0; a fixed point, so the round-trip criterion holds and it is not a blocker, but the phase should not close with a deferral whose stated reason is false (r1's own rule: a lesson line or a test comment is not a disposition — nor is a backlog line with a false trigger). → **1.30** (opus): the mark-edge clause of the ownership rule — ASCII whitespace at the outer edge of an `emphasis`, `strong` or `strikethrough` run belongs outside the mark (moved out and merged with the unmarked neighbour; a whitespace-only run loses the mark; a link's text keeps its whitespace, CommonMark allows `[b ](u)`; inline code stays opaque; the moved whitespace then falls under the line and block rules), enumerated from CommonMark's flanking rules (§6.2) rather than from the reproduction, one guard per edge and mark with first, middle and last positions, a nested-marks case, the link absence case, a typing leg that types a space at the end of every marked run over the corpus, and Sol's reproduction as a Playwright case. The backlog line is corrected by an appended line (append-only), never edited.
  - H9 **nit, taken in 1.32** (Grok 1): `packages/editor/test/position-map-editor-leg.test.ts:180` asserts `names.length === Object.keys(index).length` where `names` is `Object.keys(index).sort()` — `x === x`, the class 1.21 (F9c) and 1.26 (G8) removed from the neighbouring files; the real collectors (`blockEndChecked`, `inBlockChecked` at `:249-250`) already carry the claim. Drop the tautology.

G1 **confirmed** as fixed (the flush in `copyMarkdown`, the three spy cases green on three OSes) and its *attestation* **reopened as H4** (no real write exercised after 1.28). Unique findings (raised by one reviewer only): **Claude 5 of 6** (1, 2, 3, 5, 6), **Sol 2 of 2** (1, 2), **Grok 1 of 2** (1). Shared findings, one: H7 — Claude should-fix, Grok nit. Both caps this chain (1.23, 1.25) are the shape #review-1-r1 read from the r1 chain — the cap sits on the median of an opus fix task's work — and neither adds a reason to move before the Phase 2 boundary. Phase 1 to date: 13 of 31 loop tasks needed more than one attempt; `NO-JOURNAL` stops 10 (the nine of r1 plus 1.25). The r3 chain runs at 50 under the stub rule, as four single-concern tasks, and the boundary decision reads its transcripts by the same measure.

All four are opus: 1.29 and 1.30 change the function whose last two fixes each missed a member (1.13, 1.25 — the second at attempt 2 after a cap), 1.31 touches the core undo contract and the seam of two passed tasks, and 1.32 decides between two candidate guards by mutation.

## #022-gate-1.verify.r3h-macos-home-chord (2026-09-11, plan.1.verify.r3h.r0; principal, from the run's log; scope per DECISIONS #019 and #021, handoff 017)

- **What happened.** `scripts/gate.sh 1.verify.r3h` a1 (ci.yml run 34563964087 at `32552e0`): `test (ubuntu-latest)` and `test (windows-latest)` green on every step — 35 unit test files, `cargo test`, 79 Playwright cases each; `test (macos-latest)` green through `Cargo test` and failed at step `Playwright (e2e/web)`: 1 failed, 78 passed — `e2e/web/editor-mark-edge.spec.ts` › `a space typed at a mark edge never reaches the Markdown as a character reference` › "H8 (Sol's reproduction): `a *b* c`, the caret after `b`, one space — the space lands inside the mark and the pane is clean" (task 1.30's browser case), the first `expectClean` (spec line 78) polling for `a *b*  c\n` and receiving `a *b* c\n`, the seed's own bytes, until the 5 s poll timeout. `GATE-FAILED`, `1.verify.r3h` → `blocked`, plan request `plan.1.verify.r3h.r0`. `run.json` records `artifacts: []` (artifacts are fetched only on `conclusion === success`, #020); the `merge-logs` job ran this round and merged the three per-OS `playwright-report` uploads, but the gate did not fetch them, so the gate's own log (`.evidence/ci/1.verify.r3h/a1/workflow.log`) is the evidence. The runner conformance suite (ubuntu leg) was green.
- **Cause (principal reading; the fix task confirms it from the log and the spec before changing anything).** The case places the caret with `page.keyboard.press("ControlOrMeta+Home")` — Meta+Home on macOS — then three `ArrowRight`, then types a space. On ubuntu and windows Ctrl+Home is Blink's document-start motion. On macOS a Home, End or modifier chord in a contenteditable is resolved by the OS's key-binding layer (Cocoa scrolls on Home and End; document motion is Cmd+Arrow), so the chord did not put the caret at the document start; the caret stayed where `seedFromSource`'s click had left it, the paragraph's end, the three `ArrowRight` had nothing to traverse, the space was typed after `c`, and the trailing-whitespace clause of `stripUnparsableWhitespace` (1.25) removed it — which is exactly the received bytes (a space typed at the paragraph's *start* would be stripped to the same bytes; either way the chord was not the motion the test assumed). Two test defects, one platform accident: (1) the caret route encodes a chord whose meaning one OS decides differently — the class of #020 (a `Mod-` chord resolved from the host platform) and #021 (one platform's clipboard convention), green in the container and on two runners by accident; (2) the assertion meant to prove the space went *inside* the mark, `expect(page.locator(".ProseMirror em")).toHaveText("b ")`, normalises whitespace, so `<em>b</em>` satisfies it — and the pane assertion `a *b*  c` is the same bytes whether the space was typed inside the mark (moved out by 1.30's clause) or outside it after the delimiter, so on no runner did the case prove it built Sol's tree. The app is not implicated: 1.30's clause is exercised by its unit guards and the mark-edge corpus leg. Sol's own reproduction (`.evidence/reviews/1/r2/sol/probe.mjs` line 6) used a bare `Home` on Linux — a route Blink decides there and the OS decides on macOS. Code-caused (test code), not transient: no `gate.sh rerun`; any tree change is a `.g<n>` repair (RUNNER-SPEC §2).
- **Scope decision (explicit, so the fix task does not pick).** Four candidates: (1) branch the chord on `process.platform` (`Meta+ArrowUp` on darwin) — forbidden, a platform list; (2) widen the poll timeout — the miss is not timing; (3) replace the chord with motions Blink alone decides (arrow keys: in a one-line block `ArrowUp` reaches the block's start and `ArrowDown` its end in Blink on every OS, then the existing `ArrowRight`/`ArrowLeft` counts; or a click at a computed point of the `<em>`'s box) and make every `<em>` text assertion byte-exact (`textContent` with `toBe`; an explicit regex for the input-rule case that accepts either tree), in the failing spec file only; (4) the same replacement across every spec that uses the construct. Chosen: (3). It is strictly better than (1) and (2), not a compromise: the container proves a Blink-only route for every OS, the byte-exact assertion makes the case able to fail on the thing it claims (the tree), and no timeout hides a wrong caret. Against (4): the #021 shape scopes a gate repair to what failed; the other rendered-view uses (`editor-soft-line-breaks.spec.ts:85`, `:107`; `editor-trailing-break.spec.ts:125`, `:138`) passed on all three runners — each follows a click that already leaves the caret at the document end, so on macOS their chord is a no-op that changes nothing (line 85's assertion, "bytes unchanged", is also satisfied by a space typed at the end, so that G4 case proves nothing about the first line on macOS) — and they go to `docs/V1.1-BACKLOG.md` with a revisit trigger (below) rather than into a repair whose only proof is the next gate. The absence case in the failing file (`ControlOrMeta+End`, spec line 101) is in scope: same file, same construct, same normalised assertion. The source-view `Home`/`End` (`editor-cursor.spec.ts:36`, `editor-toggle.spec.ts:359`, `:436`) are CodeMirror's own keymap, handled in JS before the OS layer, and are not members of the class.
- **Graph change (RUNNER-SPEC §2 rule (a)), both physically in PRD §8 and `ralph/tasks.json`.** Appended after `1.verify.r3`: **`1.33`** (opus; `e2e/web/editor-mark-edge.spec.ts` only; the Blink-only caret route with the rule stated once in the doc comment, the byte-exact `<em>` assertions, four grep guards — no `Home`/`End`/`ControlOrMeta+Home|End`, no `toHaveText` on the `em` locator, no `process.platform`, no `.skip`/`.only` — the mutation that types at the paragraph's end and shows both assertions red, the reproduction quoted from the log; honest statement that the only proof is the gate) and **`1.verify.r3.g1`** (sonnet; `needsCI`, same `ci` object with `refTemplate ci/1.verify.r3.g1/a{n}`, `artifactNames` unchanged, depends on `1.33`; the promote-lessons step of `1.verify.r3` is already integrated at `32552e0` and is not repeated; `ciAcceptance` restates the three-OS claim and names `/logs/ci/1.verify.r3.g1h/accepted/`). Both texts carry the [1.29] journal-line rule (each attempt appends its own line) and the H7 wrapper note. The r3 reviewers are explicit physical tasks (#review-1-r2), so the rewire is in the PRD rows themselves: `1.10.r3a/b/c` now depend on `1.verify.r3.g1` (the generator's `needsCI` expansion makes that `1.verify.r3.g1h`), and the four `/logs/ci/1.verify.r3h/accepted/` references in their descriptions (Claude 1, Sol 1, Grok 2) read `/logs/ci/1.verify.r3.g1h/accepted/`; the `1.verify.r3` row keeps its own reference (a passed task's record); `1.10.r3d` and `1.close` unchanged; no reviewer text changes otherwise (#review-1-r0: no reviewer change inside a set). `ralph/EXPECTED_COUNT` 194 → 197 (`generate-tasks: 152 raw + 21 review-set + 24 CI gates = 197 expanded`). At integration `sync-state` marks `1.verify.r3h` `superseded`. Next human gate: `scripts/gate.sh 1.verify.r3.g1h`.
- **Model choice.** `1.33` is opus although the diff is one test file: the container cannot observe the failing platform, so a miss costs a full CI round plus another planning commit, and the task has to keep H8's guard live (the byte-exact `<em>` assertion must go red when the caret is at the paragraph's end and green on the repaired route) while choosing a route it can only argue, not run, for macOS — a wrong pick (a route that still passes through the OS layer, an assertion that still normalises, a timeout widened to make the poll pass) is invisible in the container. `1.verify.r3.g1` is sonnet, as every verifier so far.
- **For the 1.10 reviewers (r3 texts unchanged; this is for the reconciliation's reading).** Whether the repaired route reproduces Sol's tree in the container (the journal's mutation record); whether any rendered-view spec still places the caret through `Home`/`End`/a `ControlOrMeta+` chord after 1.33 (the backlog line names four); whether the merged `playwright-report` holds three OS directories; the macOS Playwright log joins the Windows one as the first unknown of every gate.
- **Reversal.** Before this planning commit integrates: `git -C .wt/plan.1.verify.r3h.r0 reset --hard phase/1` (the branch back to its cut at `32552e0`); the request stays `running`, the status `ralph.sh plan` left it in — `plan-retry` accepts only `blocked`/`integration-failed` and `plan-abandon` moves it to `abandoned`, so no ralph command returns it to `pending`; the reversal is git-only, as in #018, #020 and #021. After integration a plan request has no undo: `1.verify.r3h` stays `superseded` and any further change is another planning commit.

## #023-gate-1.verify.r3.g1h-transient-rerun-and-doctor (2026-09-11, principal; manual procedure, audited here; no planning commit)

- **What happened.** `scripts/gate.sh 1.verify.r3.g1h` a1 (ci.yml run 34572776730 at `f90ed50`): macos-latest green on everything (79 Playwright cases — 1.33's repair held), ubuntu-latest green, windows-latest 78 passed, 1 failed: `e2e/web/editor-clipboard.spec.ts:126` › "strong and emphasis round-trips through the clipboard" — `Test timeout of 30000ms exceeded` inside `load()` at `locator.setInputFiles`, "waiting for getByTestId('fixture-file')", before any assertion ran; the other worker ran cases 6–44 green during those 30 s. `GATE-FAILED`, `1.verify.r3.g1h` → `blocked`, plan request `plan.1.verify.r3.g1h.r0`. Transient, not code-caused: the file last changed at `task(1.32)` `2273cdb`, before the r3h gate at `32552e0`; the same case passed on windows-latest at every gate that carried it (`1.verify.r1h` 683 ms, `1.verify.r2h` 667 ms, `1.verify.r2.g1h` 578 ms, `1.verify.r3h` 519 ms); the tree between `32552e0` and `f90ed50` changes one e2e file, `editor-mark-edge.spec.ts`. A `.g2` repair would have had nothing to change. `scripts/gate.sh rerun 1.verify.r3.g1h` (RUNNER-SPEC §2: a rerun exists only for a transient failure; the first `gate.sh rerun` recorded in this file): a2 (run 34573685490, same SHA `f90ed50`, ref `ci/1.verify.r3.g1/a2`) — 79/79/79 on the three runners, `ACCEPT`, `accepted.json` → a2, three OS directories in both artifacts; the plan request was withdrawn by the rerun (`abandoned`, reason suffixed "withdrawn: transient rerun"), as the conformance test "transient rerun" specifies. Audit: `07:17:18Z plan-abandon … withdrawn by rerun`, `07:17:18Z gate … rerun a2 … (transient)`, `07:31:11Z transition … human-pending -> passed (a2 ACCEPT)`.
- **The runner contradicting itself.** After the ACCEPT, `ralph/ralph.sh doctor` reports `incomplete 1.verify.r3.g1h a1 (workflow failed, state not transitioned) → scripts/gate.sh 1.verify.r3.g1h --resume a1`, and `ralph.sh run` refuses on any finding (§12: "run refuses"). Cause, read from `ralph/lib/doctor.mjs` lines 90–95: for a CI gate an attempt `n` counts as recorded when the gate is `passed` with `accepted_gate_attempt === n`, or `blocked`/`superseded` with `attempts >= n`, or the attempt is `abandoned`; a1 is `rejected` (`result.json` failure, `accepted.json.attempt` 2) on a gate that is `passed` through a2, which no clause covers — the transient-rerun path (blocked → rerun → passed) was never given a clause, and its conformance test (`ralph/test/conformance.test.mjs`, "transient rerun: a2 against the same SHA…") never calls `doctor` afterwards. §2 defines an incomplete attempt as one whose `run.json` has no matching state transition; a1's transition is in the audit log (`a1 GATE-FAILED`, plan request raised at `07:13:00Z`), and the rerun could only start from it (`rerunChecks` refuses an `incomplete` last attempt). The named remedy is wrong as well as unnecessary: `--resume a1` re-enters `ciGate` with a1's `result.json` (failure), skips to step 3 and writes `blocked`/`GATE-FAILED`/`attempts: 1` over the a2 `passed` record, then raises `plan.1.verify.r3.g1h.r1` — it must not be run.
- **Why no `fix(runner)` now.** The runner executes `ralph/lib` from the host checkout on `phase/1`, so a fix has to be a commit there. `stepReview` (`ralph/lib/run.mjs`) starts a review attempt only when `phase/1`'s head equals the accepted verifier's `integrated_sha` (`f90ed50`), else `REVIEW-SHA-MISMATCH`, and the r3 reviewers have not started. Any commit on `phase/1` before `1.10.r3a/b/c` start stops the runner with no path back except another verifier task and gate. The fix waits for the phase boundary (runner idle on `phase/2`), per PRINCIPAL.md: cut to a manual procedure, no machinery on top of machinery.
- **Manual procedure (this gate, once).** `scripts/gate.sh abandon 1.verify.r3.g1h a1 --reason "rejected a1 superseded by the transient rerun a2 (ACCEPT); marked abandoned only so doctor stops reading it as incomplete (DECISIONS #023)"`. It writes `a1/abandoned.json` and an audit line and changes no task state (the gate is `passed`, not `human-pending`, so `abandonAttempt`'s only state branch does not fire); `attemptStatus` then reads a1 as `abandoned`, which `doctor` counts as recorded; `gate.sh gc` treats `rejected` and `abandoned` alike (deletes the ref). The word is a misrecord — a1 was rejected and superseded, not abandoned — and the truth is the audit log, this entry and the reason text; nothing reads a1 after this (a review reads `accepted.json` → a2). Then `ralph/ralph.sh doctor` clean, restart.
- **Phase boundary `fix(runner)` (scope decided here; lands after `1.close`, runner idle on `phase/2`).** (1) `doctor.mjs`: a `rejected` attempt `n` on a `passed` gate with `attempts > n` is recorded (a rerun can only have started from its recorded failure); (2) `ciGate` resume: refuse `--resume a<n>` when `accepted.json` names a later attempt or the gate is `passed` ("a<n> is superseded by a<m>; nothing to resume"), so doctor's remedy can never overwrite an accepted record; one conformance test per guard, and the existing transient-rerun test gains a `doctor` clean assertion at its end. The r3 reconciliation files it in `docs/V1.1-BACKLOG.md` with hard stop `2.1`.
- **For the r3 reconciliation.** `.evidence/ci/1.verify.r3.g1h/` holds a1 (rejected, then `abandoned.json` by the procedure above) and a2 (accepted); `verification_sha == implementation_sha == f90ed50` unchanged; `plan.1.verify.r3.g1h.r0` reads `abandoned` in `plan-requests.json` — by the rerun, not by `plan-abandon`; no graph change, `EXPECTED_COUNT` stays 197. This entry is on `handoff/019` (cherry-picked by the reconciliation, rotate.md step 5), which is why it carries no journal line.
- **Reversal.** The abandon: `rm .evidence/ci/1.verify.r3.g1h/a1/abandoned.json` returns doctor to its finding (the audit line stays; the log is append-only). The rerun: none — a2 is an accepted attempt with remote side effects (§2: never rolled back). This entry: `git revert` of its commit.

Evidence for the boundary: of the six opus fix tasks in the r2 and r3 chains, three ran to 49–51 turns at a1; the raise to 80 stays a proposal, taken only as a `fix(runner)` with its conformance leg on Michael's OK, read by the transcript measure. The r4 chain (1.34–1.38, 1.verify.r4) runs at 50.
- **Runner and gate observations this block.** Handoffs 017, 018 and 019 (two commits) were cherry-picked into this worktree in that order (deviation 29 procedure, sixth use): the three rotations rewrote `docs/handoffs/next-prompt.md` from the same base, so the second and third cherry-picks conflicted on that file only, each resolved to the incoming version — the result is byte-identical to `git show handoff/019:docs/handoffs/next-prompt.md`, and `DECISIONS.md` and `lessons.md` match `handoff/019` before this entry. `#023` is the one runner deviation this block (recorded there; no `fix(runner)` on `phase/1` before `1.close`). The r3 review set ran clean at attempt 1 from one snapshot; nothing was retried, re-cut or repaired; no `admin` command, no `docker/` change. `origin/phase/1` is at `4590b8d` (Michael pushes by hand, #010).
- **Graph change (RUNNER-SPEC §5.4, §3).** Appended after `1.10.r3d` in PRD §8, a linear chain: `1.34` → `1.35` → `1.36` → `1.37` → `1.38` → `1.verify.r4` (`needsCI`, `ci/1.verify.r4/a{n}`, artifacts `test-logs` and `playwright-report`, its generated gate `1.verify.r4h`) → `1.10.r4a/b/c` (depend on `1.verify.r4`, rewritten to `1.verify.r4h` by the generator) → `1.10.r4d` (this role again, a fresh session); `1.close` now depends on `1.10.r4d`. `1.34` depends on `1.10.r3d` so the chain starts when this commit integrates. `ralph/EXPECTED_COUNT` 197 → the count the generator prints (recorded in the journal). Every fix task's text carries the r1 rule (a defect found outside its scope gets a backlog line with a revisit trigger and a hard stop in the same commit), the journal-line-per-attempt sentence and H7(b)'s wrapper sentence. Five fix tasks is the honest size: two blockers in two code areas (the strip's mark-edge clause at an atom; block-level `spread` after a structural edit), one regression of the r2 chain's own fix in the first area with its pre-existing sibling (the link predicate), one Required test gap in the position-map family, one Required test-only repair of four e2e cases with the guard #022 promised — each one iteration with its file list named. All five are opus: 1.34 and 1.35 change the function whose last three fixes each missed a member and where two of the last three attempts capped; 1.36 changes the block conversion of 1.1 and must keep 55 fixtures byte-identical; 1.37 and 1.38 have to be able to fail on a thing the container cannot show (a wrong map, a wrong caret on macOS).
- **Promote-lessons for `1.verify.r4` (RUNNER-SPEC §5.5), carried verbatim in that task's text, three candidates weighed there.** (a) **Promote**: each attempt appends its own `- [<id>] ` journal line; completing an earlier attempt's stub and appending nothing is `NO-JOURNAL` even with DONE printed (steps 2 and 4 of `## Every iteration`; discharged by the runner's stop-check conformance test that counts journal lines per attempt, named in the promoting journal entry). (b) **Promote**: an e2e places the rendered caret only through motions Blink alone decides — arrow keys, or a click at a computed point — never a Home/End key or a `ControlOrMeta+` chord in a contenteditable; a mark's text is asserted byte-exact (#022 wording; discharged by 1.33's in-file grep guards and 1.38's tree-wide guard). (c) **Not an agent rule**: the `[0.0]` doctor lesson on `handoff/019` (a doctor remedy is read against `gate.mjs` before anyone runs it; every conformance scenario that ends in a new state ends with a doctor-clean assertion) addresses the principal and the runner's conformance suite, which a verify task cannot edit (zero-dependency, `docs/lessons.md` and the journal only, plus the agent files); it lands in the boundary `fix(runner)` #023 scopes (the doctor-clean assertion is one of its guards) and in `docs/PRINCIPAL.md` at the boundary; the verify task records that reading, promotes nothing for it.
- **Backlog** lines appended to `docs/V1.1-BACKLOG.md`: #023's boundary `fix(runner)` (hard stop `2.1`); I7's conditional fetch-on-failure candidate (Michael's OK; hard stop `2.1`) and the flake-signature line with its trigger (hard stop `2.verify`); the correction of the `[1.30]` break-inside-a-mark line (reachable now, taken in 1.34); the `[1.30]` Unicode line decided (I10, re-pointed at Phase 3); the `[1.32]` tautology line dispositioned (1.34); the `[plan.1.verify.r3h.r0]` chord line dispositioned (1.38); the class behind I2 (every parsed block attribute re-derived after a structural edit) for Phase 2 planning beside the property test (hard stop `2.verify`); the turn-budget line's r3-chain counts (evidence only; the decision unchanged); the reviewer-record counts for the boundary decision.
- **Reversal.** Before this commit integrates: `git -C .wt/1.10.r3d reset --hard phase/1` (the task stays `principal-pending`, the status `run` left it in; the cherry-picked handoffs return to `handoff/017`, `handoff/018` and `handoff/019`, which are not deleted until after `1.close`; the review copies and the I9 redaction are gone with it). After integration a reconciliation has no undo: its verdict is written to `.evidence/reviews/1/r3/verdict`, the r4 chain is in the graph, and any change is a further planning commit.

## #024-gate-1.verify.r4h-vertical-arrow-caret-route (2026-09-12, plan.1.verify.r4h.r0; principal, from the run's log; scope per DECISIONS #019, #021 and #022, handoff 020)

- **What happened.** `scripts/gate.sh 1.verify.r4h` a1 (ci.yml run 34685866034 at `4c133e0`): every unit test file (37, 2,798 vitest passed plus the one `it.fails` case 1.37 pinned for its `[1.37]` backlog line), `cargo test` and the lint step green on all three runners; macos-latest green on all 83 Playwright cases; `test (ubuntu-latest)` and `test (windows-latest)` each 82 passed, 1 failed — the same case on both: `e2e/web/editor-soft-line-breaks.spec.ts:119` › "G4: a space typed at the end of the first line of a wrapped paragraph carries no entity" (task 1.38's repair of the former `:85` chord), red on the **caret precondition** 1.38 added at Sol's request: expected `{ kind: "text", text: "alpha\nbeta gamma", offset: 5 }`, received offset **8** on ubuntu and **16** on windows, the poll timing out at spec line 135 before the space was typed. `GATE-FAILED`, `1.verify.r4h` → `blocked`, plan request `plan.1.verify.r4h.r0`. `run.json` records `artifacts: []` (fetched only on success, #020); the gate's own log (`.evidence/ci/1.verify.r4h/a1/workflow.log`) is the evidence.
- **Cause (principal reading; the fix task confirms it from the log and the spec before changing anything).** 1.38 placed the caret with `toDocumentStart(page, 2)` — `ArrowUp` pressed once per visual line — on the claim, stated in the file's doc comment, that in Blink `ArrowUp` on a block's first visual line moves to the editable root's start on every OS, then five `ArrowRight`. The received offsets say otherwise: on ubuntu the two presses left the caret at offset 3 (inside `alpha`, at the x-position the seed's caret had on the line below — a visual-line motion keeps its x), on windows at offset 11 (the caret never left the second line, whose wrapping and metrics differ on that runner), and the five `ArrowRight` added five. The container (headless Chromium, its own fonts and width) and macos-latest happened to land at 0. 1.33's rule was stated for a **one-line** block, where the vertical arrow has no line to move to and Blink goes to the content's edge; 1.38 extended it to two-line blocks, where `ArrowUp`/`ArrowDown` are visual-line motions whose landing depends on the caret's x, the wrapping and the font metrics — Blink decides them, but not from the document alone, so the container proves nothing about another runner's geometry. The location assertion did exactly what it was added for: it failed on the wrong placement instead of letting "bytes unchanged" pass. The app is not implicated (the clause under test is 1.25's, exercised by unit guards and green on macOS). Code-caused (test code), not transient: no `gate.sh rerun`; a tree change is a `.g<n>` repair (RUNNER-SPEC §2).
- **Scope decision (explicit, so the fix task does not pick).** Candidates: (1) more `ArrowUp` presses or a per-OS count — the landing depends on x and metrics, not on the count; (2) a click at a computed point of the first line — a valid Blink-only route, but a coordinate computed from the rendered box is one more geometry dependency; (3) **counted horizontal motions from an asserted anchor**: the block's end, where `seedFromSource`'s click leaves the caret on every OS (the G3 case in the same file types there and is green on all three runners at every gate since 1.25; 1.38's own end-of-block assertions in the hard-break, H1 and H5 cases passed on all three), asserted first as `{ text: "alpha\nbeta gamma", offset: 16 }`, then `ArrowLeft` pressed `"beta gamma".length + 1` times (the LF is one character in the text node under `white-space: pre-wrap`; a `<br>` is one caret position) to offset 5, then the existing offset-5 assertion — character motions are metric-independent; (4) the same replacement in `editor-trailing-break.spec.ts`, whose `toDocumentEnd` (`ArrowDown`) sits before an end-of-block assertion that passed on all three runners. Chosen: (3), for the whole failing file — the failing case, and the hard-break case's `toDocumentEnd(page, 2)` in the same file, which is the same construct one press away from the same miss (it passed because the click's caret was already at the end, the #022 accident again); the file's doc comment loses the vertical-arrow claim and states the anchor-and-count rule once. (3) is strictly better than (1) and (2), not a compromise: the anchor is the one position every case in the suite already relies on and asserts, and the count is a property of the text, not of a runner. Against (4): the #021 shape scopes a gate repair to what failed; the trailing-break file's two uses passed on all three runners **with** an end-of-block assertion after them (1.38's addition), so they are proven for this geometry, and they go to `docs/V1.1-BACKLOG.md` with a trigger (a red landing assertion on any runner → the anchor-and-count route). `tests/no-caret-chords-in-e2e.test.ts` is unchanged (it forbids chords and Home/End; vertical arrows are not its subject) and stays green.
- **Graph change (RUNNER-SPEC §2 rule (a)), both physically in PRD §8 and `ralph/tasks.json`.** Appended after `1.verify.r4`: **`1.39`** (opus; `e2e/web/editor-soft-line-breaks.spec.ts` only; the anchor-and-count route in both cases that place the caret, the end-of-block anchor asserted before the count, the doc comment rewritten, the mutation that shortens the count by one and shows the location assertion red, the reproduction quoted from the log; honest statement that the only proof is the gate) and **`1.verify.r4.g1`** (sonnet; `needsCI`, same `ci` object with `refTemplate ci/1.verify.r4.g1/a{n}`, `artifactNames` unchanged, depends on `1.39`; the promote-lessons step of `1.verify.r4` is already integrated at `4c133e0` and is not repeated; `ciAcceptance` restates the three-OS claim and names `/logs/ci/1.verify.r4.g1h/accepted/`). Both texts carry the journal-line-per-attempt sentence and the H7 wrapper note. The r4 reviewers are explicit physical tasks (#review-1-r3), so the rewire is in the PRD rows: `1.10.r4a/b/c` now depend on `1.verify.r4.g1` (the generator's `needsCI` expansion makes that `1.verify.r4.g1h`), and the one `/logs/ci/1.verify.r4h/accepted/` reference in each description reads `/logs/ci/1.verify.r4.g1h/accepted/`; the `1.verify.r4` row keeps its own reference (a passed task's record); `1.10.r4d` and `1.close` unchanged; no reviewer text changes otherwise (#review-1-r0). `ralph/EXPECTED_COUNT` 208 → the count the generator prints (the journal line records it). At integration `sync-state` marks `1.verify.r4h` `superseded`. Next human gate: `scripts/gate.sh 1.verify.r4.g1h`.
- **Model choice.** `1.39` is opus for 1.33's reason: the container cannot observe the failing runners, a miss costs a CI round and another planning commit, and the task has to keep the location assertion live while choosing a route it can only argue for the other geometries. `1.verify.r4.g1` is sonnet, as every verifier.
- **For the 1.10 reviewers (r4 texts unchanged beyond the rewire).** Whether any rendered-view caret placement in `e2e/web/` still relies on a vertical arrow to reach a block's edge in a block that renders more than one line (the trailing-break file's two `toDocumentEnd` uses, on the backlog); whether the anchor-and-count route reproduces the first-line tree in the container (the journal's mutation record); the `it.fails` case in `position-map-editor-leg.test.ts` (1.37's pinned known defect, `[1.37]` backlog line) read against CLAUDE.md's "never `.skip()`": a pinned expected failure that goes red when the defect is fixed is the opposite of a skip, and the reconciliation dispositions the defect itself.
- **Reversal.** Before this planning commit integrates: `git -C .wt/plan.1.verify.r4h.r0 reset --hard phase/1` (the branch back to its cut at `4c133e0`); the request stays `running`, the status `ralph.sh plan` left it in — `plan-retry` accepts only `blocked`/`integration-failed` and `plan-abandon` moves it to `abandoned`, so no ralph command returns it to `pending`; the reversal is git-only, as in #018, #020, #021 and #022. After integration a plan request has no undo: `1.verify.r4h` stays `superseded` and any further change is another planning commit.

## #025-reviewer-retry-reads-sibling-reports (2026-09-12, principal; review set 1.10 r4; manual procedure, audited here; no planning commit)

- **What happened.** `1.10.r4a/b/c` started together at 10:13:49Z from one snapshot (`6c0e601`). Claude and Sol passed at 10:48:13Z (both FAIL, 1 blocker each). Grok's run died after 13 model calls (1.47M input tokens, 1.28M cached) on `API error (status 402 Payment Required): Grok Build usage balance exhausted`; the entrypoint's transcript fallback then wrote 4,101 lines of tool output as `report.md`, no `status.json`, exit 2 → `1.10.r4c` `blocked`, `STUCK 1.10.r4 (1 reviewer(s) failed; ralph.sh retry <id>)`. Michael reset the Grok Build usage (his one reset; he will not pay for Grok; on a recurrence he checks the usage and decides how to proceed — recorded as his decision, 2026-09-12). The principal moved the failed attempt's files aside (`report.a1-402.md`, `transcript.a1-402.log` — the runner does not clear a reviewer's directory on retry and `grok-review` skips its fallback when `report.md` is non-empty, so the stale file would have failed the retry too), ran `ralph/ralph.sh retry 1.10.r4c` (`blocked → pending`, attempts 0) and restarted; `stepReview` started Grok alone from the same snapshot at 11:04:55Z; it passed at 11:14:56Z (FAIL, 1 blocker / 3 should-fix / 2 nits; 19 model calls; 10 minutes against the 35 of the parallel run).
- **The runner contradicting itself.** RUNNER-SPEC §5 has three *independent* reviewers, and the independence rests on nothing but the parallel start: every reviewer mounts the whole attempt directory `/logs/reviews/<phase>/<attempt>/` (the prompt sends it to `/logs` for the gate evidence), and a sibling's `report.md` is simply not there yet when all three start together. A single-reviewer retry (`retry` + `stepReview`'s `todo` filter) starts one reviewer after the others have finished, with their reports present. Grok's r4 transcript reads `/logs/reviews/1/r4/claude/report.md` at its 16th tool call (transcript line 242, before it wrote a word of its own; 7 reads of each sibling's `report.md`, 3 of each `status.json`), its report has 17 sentences identical to Claude's and 4 to Sol's out of 184, its findings 1–6 are Claude's 1–6 in order with the same `file:line` citations and its three risks are Claude's, and its inputs line does not name either report. At r3 (parallel) Grok's only `/logs/reviews/1/r3/{claude,sol}` hit was a read of `ralph/tasks.json`'s reviewer rows. The report is not independent evidence; no rule was broken by Grok (nothing forbade the read) and none by the retry command (it did what §3 says); the gap is the spec's.
- **Manual procedure (until the boundary `fix(runner)`).** Before `ralph/ralph.sh retry <set>.<attempt><x>` for a single reviewer: (1) move the failed attempt's own `report.md` and `transcript.log` aside under their directory with an attempt suffix (evidence kept, the fallback guard cleared); (2) move the sibling reviewer directories out of the attempt directory — `mkdir -p .evidence/reviews/<phase>/<attempt>.held && mv .evidence/reviews/<phase>/<attempt>/{<sibling>,<sibling>} .evidence/reviews/<phase>/<attempt>.held/` — the metadata files (`phase_base_sha`, `verifier_id`, `verification_sha`, `implementation_sha`) stay; (3) retry, restart, wait for the reviewer to pass; (4) move the siblings back before the reconciliation starts (`<set>.<attempt>d` reads all three). Reversal of each step is the opposite `mv`. If a Grok run fails on a 402 again: stop, report the usage numbers to Michael, and wait for his decision — never retry on the principal's own initiative.
- **For the r4 reconciliation (`1.10.r4d`).** Grok's r4 report is copied verbatim to `docs/reviews/phase-1-r4-grok.md` as every report is (the record is the record), and `#review-1-r4` treats it as **derived, not independent**: its unique findings are 0 by construction and are not counted; its ratings are not entered into the shared-findings low-rater table; the reviewer record states that attempt r4 has two independent reports (Claude, Sol) and one derived one, with this entry as the reason; its verdict does not enter the PASS/FAIL count (the verdict rule of RUNNER-SPEC §5.4 is met by the two independent FAILs and the reconciliation's own reproductions). No reviewer change inside the set (#review-1-r0); whether Grok reviews r5 (the budget, the contamination) is Michael's decision at the point the r5 texts are written — the reconciliation writes the r5 reviewer rows as the generator expects (three reviewers) and records the question for him.
- **Phase boundary `fix(runner)` (scope decided here; lands after `1.close`, runner idle on `phase/2`; hard stop `2.1`).** `stepReview`, when it starts a strict subset of a review set (`todo.length < 3`), gives the started reviewer a view of the attempt directory that holds only its own subdirectory and the four metadata files (a bind mount of a per-reviewer view, or the sibling directories renamed under the lock for the run's duration and restored before the state transition), and the entrypoints' `check_report` refuses a report whose transcript read a sibling's `report.md`; one conformance test per guard (a retried reviewer cannot read a sibling's report; a full-set start is unchanged; the siblings are back before `passed` is written), and the existing retry test ends with `doctor` clean.
- **Reversal.** This entry: `git revert` of its commit. The retry and the reset: none (an attempt and the one reset are consumed; the record above is the truth).

## #review-1-r4 (2026-09-12, task 1.10.r4d; principal reconciliation of review attempt r4 at implementation_sha 6c0e601a49609036f02678436db4b380d340efb7, verifier 1.verify.r4.g1, gate 1.verify.r4.g1h ACCEPT a1, run 34687540740; an autonomous session — no decision in this entry is Michael's except the one #025 records, and every conditional item stays conditional on his OK)

verdict: FAIL

- **Reviewer outcomes** (`.evidence/reviews/1/r4/<reviewer>/status.json`; reports byte-identical in `docs/reviews/phase-1-r4-{claude,sol,grok}.md`, `cmp` clean, 70 / 53 / 75 lines). Claude (Opus, cold build in `/scratch/claude`, six throwaway vitest probe files including a 61,682-transaction sweep of every keystroke-shaped single transaction at every inline position of every fixture and an 11,060-pair sweep, one throwaway Playwright spec, one guard mutation, one spec mutation, 2,798/2,798 + 1 expected fail, 83/83 Playwright, 83/83 runner tests, `num_turns` 110): **FAIL**, 1 blocker / 3 should-fix / 5 nits, 9 findings. Sol (Codex, cold build in `/scratch/sol`, the unchanged r3 fuzz script — 14,579 valid single transactions over 57 indexed fixtures, one stored mismatch in `link-in-emphasis.md`, zero in `nested-lists.md` against r3's 25 of 25 —, `extra-probe.mjs` 294 transactions with zero mismatches, `strike-probe.mjs` and `cursor-probe.mjs` through the real clipboard and the toggle, isolated `latency.spec.ts --workers=1`, artifact hashing, credential scan, caret-guard mutation 1 failed / 41 passed): **FAIL**, 1 / 1 / 0, 2 findings. Grok (`1.10.r4c`, **derived, not independent — #025**): the parallel run died on a `402 Payment Required` after 13 model calls; the retry started alone at 11:04:55Z from the same snapshot with Claude's and Sol's finished reports present and read them before writing (#025's count: 7 reads of each sibling's `report.md`, from its 16th tool call; this reconciliation's `grep -c` over its transcript: 4 lines naming each sibling's report; no cold build, no browser, `num_turns` 19): `status.json` says **FAIL**, 1 / 3 / 2, 6 findings, and its findings 1–6 are Claude's 1–6 in order with Claude's `file:line` citations. Per #025 its verdict is outside the count, its unique findings are 0 by construction, and its ratings enter no low-rater table; the copy is in `docs/reviews/phase-1-r4-grok.md` as every report is. Both independent reviewers verified both accepted artifact digests (`test-logs` `ca4fafe860be…` 116,413 bytes / 10 files; `playwright-report` `2d476ac1fc02…` 2,020,449 bytes / 3 files; MATCH), `verification_sha == implementation_sha == 6c0e601`, `verifier_id 1.verify.r4.g1`, three OS directories in each artifact, 2,798 vitest + 1 expected fail and 83 Playwright (17 spec files, counted from the directory) per OS. No human gate ran in this attempt; no record is copied here.
- **Verdict per PRD §7 criterion.** Dev route with the bar: pass (2 of 2 independent). Michael writes ~1,000 words and toggles to source: pass on the record (Claude) / unverifiable for the word count (Sol); F16 stands, unchanged. **The copied Markdown round-trips: fail**, twice, one family, each reproduced by this reconciliation headlessly through the same conversion the Copy button runs (`mdastToPM` → one `tr.delete` → `pmToMdast` → `format`, in a detached scratch worktree at `6c0e601`, throwaway vitest files, the worktree removed before the commit): (J1, Claude's route) `Alpha ~~beta.~~ gamma delta\n`, the space before `gamma` deleted → `Alpha ~~beta.~~gamma delta\n`, whose `format(parse(·))` is `Alpha \~\~beta.\~\~gamma delta\n` and whose parse holds no `delete`; the same with a digit outside (`x ~~a.~~1\n`), with inline code inside (`x ~~`a`~~b\n` → `x \~\~`a`\~\~b\n`), and on the opening side (`Alpha~~(beta)~~ gamma\n` → `Alpha\~\~(beta)\~\~ gamma\n`); (J1, Sol's route) `~~a [b ](u)~~ c\n`, the space before `c` deleted → `~~a [b ](u)~~c\n` → `\~\~a [b ](u)\~\~c\n`, no `delete`, the same two strings as Sol's real-clipboard log; (J1, the corpus) `fixtures/markdown/link-in-emphasis.md`, the one space after `](essay.md)~~` deleted → not a fixed point, `delete` count 1 → 0. The emphasis and strong twins of Claude's route come back as `Alpha *beta.*&#x67;amma delta\n` and `Alpha **beta.**&#x67;amma delta\n` — fixed points whose parse keeps the mark and whose entity decodes to the letter — because `mdast-util-to-markdown`'s own handlers encode the outside letter (`encode-info.js`: letter outside, punctuation inside → `outside: true`) and `mdast-util-gfm-strikethrough`'s `handleDelete` writes `~~` blind; GFM's tokenizer (`micromark-extension-gfm-strikethrough/lib/syntax.js`: `_close = !before || before === punctuation && Boolean(after)`) then cannot close the run. The parser's own tree is not safe either: `parse("~~a.~~&#x62;\n")` holds one `delete` and `format` of it is `~~a.~~b\n`, which reparses to none — a Phase 0 invariant-A gap the corpus has no fixture for. Outline verdict recorded: pass (2 of 2). Full suite and every e2e/web spec green on three OSes at the implementation SHA, digests matched: pass (2 of 2; a1, no rerun). Zero dependencies beyond PRD §4: pass (2 of 2; `check-deps` 61 npm + 4 cargo allowlisted in both cold builds and on all three runners; `pnpm why @codemirror/commands` empty in both and in this reconciliation's scratch install). Phase check, typing latency p95 with 10k words under the 1.17 protocol: **pass on a quiet machine** — rendered pane p95 9.8 ms (Claude, isolated, n=120) and 10.1 ms (Sol, isolated); 24.7 / 23.9 / 12.9 ms on the ubuntu / windows / macos runners of the accepted gate, reported separately as G10 asked; the source `commit` line (G9) 56.1 / 56.5 ms isolated, evidence only; the 25× decision (#review-1-r1) stands. Two independent FAILs on one blocker family, reproduced here → **FAIL** (RUNNER-SPEC §5.4; Grok's derived FAIL is not part of that count). `cargo test` is still vacuous (0/0/0 on three OSes; the Rust surface is Phase 2).
- **Findings, severity as reconciled, disposition** (fix tasks appended in this commit, physically in PRD §8 and `ralph/tasks.json`; J-numbers follow I; "1.13/1.30 territory" means the defect lives in a passed task's file — the fix is a new task, never an edit of the passed record, DECISIONS #016; Grok's numbers are given for the record only, as derived):
  - J1 **blocker** (Claude 1, Sol 1; Grok 1 derived — the one shared finding at blocker, agreed): the serializer half of the flanking property. `packages/core/src/format.ts` takes `gfmStrikethroughToMarkdown()` as-is, and its `handleDelete` has none of the `encodeInfo` logic the built-in `emphasis`/`strong` handlers have, so a `delete` run whose edge character is punctuation (`.`, `)`, `` ` ``, `]`) directly beside unmarked alphanumeric text serialises to `~~` delimiters GFM can neither open nor close — `format` writes bytes whose parse holds no `delete`, Copy Markdown reports "Copied Markdown", and the rendered view still shows the strike. Reproduced above for the closing side (letter and digit outside; punctuation, inline code and link inside), the opening side, Sol's link member and the corpus route. The editor's strip is **not** the cause: `giveUpEdges` (`schema.ts`) moves whitespace and breaks by design and has nothing to move here; Sol's member is I3's own rule doing its job — `outermostMark` writes the strikethrough outside a link with edge whitespace, so `)` is the delimiter's neighbour — while the same tree without the link's edge space (`x ~~[a](u)~~b`) round-trips as `x [~~a~~](u)b` because the link is written outermost by rank. Every Phase 1 fix of this family (F1, G4, H1, H8, I1, I3/I4) landed in the strip on the assumption that `format` is right about flanking; one extension handler is not (Claude's risk 1). → **1.40** (opus; `packages/core`, Phase 0 territory, #016): a `delete` handler in `format.ts` beside `opaqueHandlers()` that mirrors `mdast-util-to-markdown/lib/handle/emphasis.js`'s use of `encodeInfo` for the `~` marker (encode the outside letter when the inside is punctuation, on both sides; the letter-inside and whitespace cases as `encode-info.js` already classifies them for GFM's `~`), stated once in its doc comment; one guard per side × neighbour class (letter, digit, punctuation, whitespace) × inner class (punctuation, letter, inline code, link), each asserting the bytes, the parsed `delete` count and the parse∘format fixed point; the invariant-A gap closed by the same handler and pinned by a fixture `strikethrough-punctuation.md` (`~~a.~~ b`, `a ~~(b)~~`, `~~a.~~&#x62;`'s tree) with its canonical sibling and index entry so the identity corpus covers the shape; asserted able to fail with the package's own handler restored under a test-local formatter. → **1.41** (sonnet, test-only; `packages/editor/test/typing-legs.ts`, `editor-fixed-point.test.ts`, one e2e spec): a corpus leg titled for **the mark's neighbour** that deletes the unmarked whitespace on each side of every flanking-marked run over every fixture (and, for the certain case, types `.` at the run's end first), asserting the fixed point and that the parsed output keeps the input's mark count; Claude's and Sol's reproductions as Playwright cases with Blink-only caret motion (a click at the paragraph's end asserted as the anchor, counted `ArrowLeft`, one `Backspace`, Copy Markdown, the spy's bytes a fixed point and the `<del>` count in the parse asserted). The corpus leg the r3 fix tasks added (`deleteToEveryMarkedRunEnd`) deletes to the marked run's end; none of the five legs deleted the whitespace *between* a marked run and its unmarked neighbour, which is the route here (both independent reports say so in the same words).
  - J2 **should-fix, taken** (Claude 2; Grok 2 derived): `needsBlankLine` (`schema.ts`, the paragraph clause) misses the pair *paragraph then setext heading*: `mdast-util-to-markdown/lib/join.js` writes a blank line after a paragraph before a heading `formatHeadingAsSetext` chooses (depth ≤ 2 with a `\n` in a literal or a `break` in its text) whatever the item's flag says, so a tight item `[paragraph, heading("h\nx", depth 1), list]` serialises as `- a\n\n  h\n  x\n  =\n  - i\n` while `parse` reads the item as spread and writes `…=\n\n  - i\n` — reproduced here (`fixed: false`). `list-spread.test.ts`'s `KINDS.heading` is ATX depth 2 with no break, so its 225 pairs cannot see it — I2's class, one pair short. Route: the heading input rule at the start of a hard-wrapped second paragraph of a list item followed by a nested list; reachable, not ordinary, which is why Claude did not mark it Required and why it is taken all the same: the criterion fails on that route, and the fix is one clause plus two enumeration entries. → **1.42** (sonnet): in the paragraph clause, `right.type === "heading" && right.depth <= 2` and the same predicate `format-heading-as-setext.js` uses (a `\r?\n|\r` in any literal descendant, or a `break`); the two setext variants added to `KINDS` so the pair test asserts them by construction; one guard with the nested list present and absent, each asserting bytes and the fixed point; every fixture byte-identical through the identity legs. 1.36 territory.
