## #026-task-1.40-entity-assertion-and-position-map-scope (2026-09-12, principal, during the r5 chain; STUCK 1.40 after three attempts on a task-text conflict the agent correctly refused to decide; manual procedure, audited here; no planning commit — the graph is unchanged)

- **What happened.** `1.40` attempt 1 built the whole task (the `delete` handler in `packages/core/src/format.ts` with `encodeInfo` ported from the installed `encode-info.js`, `strikethrough-flanking.test.ts` 40/40 with the 32 side × neighbour × inner guards and the registration test, the fixture `strikethrough-punctuation.md` with its canonical sibling and index entry) and was cut at the 50-turn cap (`num_turns` 51, stub present, no stop signal). Attempts 2 and 3 (23 and 13 turns) verified it green — lint, cargo, e2e 83/83, 2,857 unit tests passed — and stopped without DONE on **7 reds, all in `packages/editor/test/editor-fixed-point.test.ts`**, writing the conflict to the journal and not deciding it (CLAUDE.md: a task that conflicts with the rules stops and does not decide). The runner then wrote `STUCK 1.40` (`blocked`, attempts 3). The agent's reading is right: the task text's three sentences cannot all hold — the fixture as specified (its third paragraph `~~a.~~&#x62;` holds a numeric character reference by construction, the invariant-A case), "the editor-fixed-point suite green unchanged", and a scope that excludes `packages/editor/test/**`.
- **The conflict, read.** Six legs of `editor-fixed-point.test.ts` assert `not.toMatch(ENTITY)` (`/&#x?[0-9a-fA-F]+;/`, line 113, widened by 1.34 over the family) over the whole output bytes, and the mark-edge leg asserts "only spaces differ" against the canonical text. Both were written when every numeric character reference in the output was a defect: the family's members (`&#x20;` for a typed space, `\&#xA;` for a break) were references the *parsed text kept* because the editor's tree was unparsable. A reference the serializer writes for the **outside neighbour** (`&#x62;` for the `b` beside `~~a.~~`) is the opposite case: it decodes to the editor's own text, the parse holds the mark, and the built-in `emphasis`/`strong` handlers have always written it (`x *a.*b` → `x *a.*&#x62;` at the branch base) — the legs never met it because no fixture held a letter beside a punctuation edge. The rule the r3 reconciliation's lesson states is the right one and the legs' instrument is stricter than the rule: "the output's parsed tree keeps the node kinds the input had, and no *text node* holds a numeric character reference". Trimming the fixture (the agent's option b) would hide the instrument's error and lose the invariant-A shape from the identity corpus.
- **Decision 1 (scope of 1.40 widened by this entry; the task text in `ralph/tasks.json` is immutable and is not edited).** 1.40 may edit `packages/editor/test/editor-fixed-point.test.ts` (and `typing-legs.ts` only if a helper is needed) for one thing: the entity assertion moves from the output bytes to the rule — for every leg and guard that asserts `not.toMatch(ENTITY)` over `format(root)` or `out`, assert instead that (i) no text node of `parse(out)` matches `ENTITY` (the `textValues` form the file already uses at lines 862 and 1388), and (ii) the output decoded — every `&#x…;`/`&#…;` reference replaced by its character — equals the editor's own text for that block where the leg compares text, which is what the mark-edge leg's "only spaces differ" becomes (a comparison of decoded texts); the `ENTITY` constant, its doc comment and the file's own comment about the family are reworded to say which references are forbidden (a reference the parsed text keeps) and which are the serializer's correct output (one that decodes to the neighbour); one guard in the file pins the distinction with the emphasis twin `x *a.*b` → `x *a.*&#x62;` (a fixed point whose parsed text is `b`) beside the family's `&#x20;` member (red under the old instrument, red under the new one). The fixture keeps its three paragraphs. The acceptance sentence "the editor-fixed-point suite green unchanged" reads, for 1.40, "green, with the entity assertion narrowed as DECISIONS #026 says and every other assertion unchanged in meaning". Everything else in 1.40's text stands.
- **Decision 2 (the second instance, `formatWithMap`).** Attempt 1 found that `placeChildren` (`packages/core/src/positions.ts`, task 1.2) reports a text node as `unresolved` when `containerPhrasing` encoded its edge character after the node's handler returned (`container-phrasing.js`'s `encodeAfter` / `encodingInfo.before`): the emission `b` is not in the parent's output, `&#x62;` is. Pre-existing for `*a.*&#x62;` and `**a.**&#x62;` at the branch base; the cursor map falls to its block clause for that text node — J3's class on the toggle path. Attempt 1 pinned it as `it.fails` in `positions-corpus.test.ts` with a `[1.40, found outside scope]` backlog line, which is the task text's own rule and is accepted for 1.40 (its attempt budget is spent on the handler). The fix is **taken in 1.43**, the cursor-map task, whose scope is widened by this entry to `packages/core/src/positions.ts` — `placeChildren` looks the child's emission up by its encoded form when `indexOf` of the literal misses (the rewrite is deterministic: the first or last character replaced by a hexadecimal character reference), and the spelling table carries the reference the way it carries an escape — and `packages/core/test/positions-corpus.test.ts` (the `it.fails` case and its fixture list removed, the fixture folded into the ordinary "nothing is unresolved" case). 1.43's acceptance sentence "`pnpm test`'s summary shows no expected fail" already requires it.
- **Instrument.** This entry on `handoff/023` (cut from `phase/1` at `cef59c2`; cherry-picked by the `1.10.r5d` reconciliation, rotate.md step 5), and one `chore(1.40): principal decision …` commit on `task/1.40` in `.wt/1.40` carrying the two decisions verbatim as a `[1.40]` line in `docs/lessons.md` (the sanctioned shape of #review-1-r3 for a principal commit inside a task branch; `docs/lessons.md` only; squashed into `task(1.40)`, so 1.43's agent reads the same line from the tree). Then `ralph/ralph.sh retry 1.40` (`blocked → pending`, attempts 0; the branch and worktree are kept — `start` is idempotent when both exist, `ralph/lib/integrate.mjs`), and the restart. Attempt 1 of the retry inherits attempts 1–3's commits and their journal lines; it appends its own.
- **Why not a planning commit.** The graph is unchanged; a planning commit rewrites tasks, and the conflict is between one task's sentences, resolved by choosing which sentence yields. The pattern is #023's and #025's: cut to a manual procedure and record it. The class-level lesson — a fix task whose new fixture reaches a shape a neighbouring suite's instrument forbids by over-breadth will stop on the conflict; the reconciliation that writes such a task reads the neighbouring suites' invariants for the shape the fixture introduces — goes to `docs/lessons.md` on the task branch with the decision.
- **Reversal.** This entry: `git revert` of its commit on `handoff/023` (or `git branch -D handoff/023` before it is cherry-picked). The lesson commit: `git -C .wt/1.40 reset --hard HEAD~1` before the retry's first attempt commits. The retry: none once an attempt has run (an attempt is consumed); before it, `ralph.sh` has no command that returns `pending` to `blocked` — the state file is not hand-edited, so the reversal of `retry` is to let the task run.

## #027-grok-reviews-r5-and-the-session-limit-429 (2026-09-12, Michael's decision, recorded by the principal during the r5 chain; no planning commit)

- **Michael's decision (2026-09-12, his words: "session limit reset, grok also has usage limits reset, we're good to proceed as normal").** Grok reviews r5. The open question #review-1-r4 recorded for him (whether Grok reviews r5 at all — the budget, and #025's contamination on any single-reviewer retry) is closed: the `1.10.r5c` row stands as written; no planning commit. #025's manual procedure for a single-reviewer retry and its rule that a Grok 402 is never retried on the principal's initiative are unchanged.
- **The session-limit 429 (evidence, no decision).** `1.41`'s retry attempt 2 (sonnet) ended at 16 turns with `api_error_status 429`, `"You've hit your session limit · resets 2:30pm (UTC)"`, exit 1, before the agent wrote its journal stub; the runner read it as `NO-JOURNAL 1.41` (`running`, attempts 2) — one attempt of three consumed by the subscription's session limit, not by the agent. Michael reset it. The stop is per RUNNER-SPEC; the restart is the same command, no `retry` (the task is `running`). Filed for the boundary with the turn-budget evidence: an attempt whose transcript ends in a 429 is a consumed attempt the runner cannot tell from an agent's `NO-JOURNAL`; a `fix(runner)` candidate (read `api_error_status` from the result line and stop with a named signal instead of consuming the attempt) is proposed with this one instance as the evidence, conditional on Michael's OK, hard stop `2.1`. Until then, on a 429 in a task transcript: do not restart into the limit; wait for the reset Michael names, then the same restart command.
- **1.41's retry, audited.** After `STUCK 1.41` (three attempts capped at 51 with the work complete and green on the branch — leg, two Playwright cases, e2e 85/85, lint and unit suites green in the third transcript), the three logs were moved aside as `.evidence/tasks/1.41/{1.a1-capped,2.a2-capped,3.a3-capped}.log` (the retry of `1.40` had overwritten its capped attempt-1 transcript, because `retry` resets the attempt counter and the runner writes `<attempt>.log`; the evidence rule from #025 now covers task logs too), a `chore(1.41): principal lesson` commit named the remaining steps, and `ralph.sh retry 1.41`. Its attempt 1 recorded both mutations red and re-ran the suites, capped again before the journal; attempt 2 the 429 above; a second lesson tells attempt 3 to write the record and print DONE without running a suite (the runner re-runs the whole suite on the squashed candidate, RUNNER-SPEC §4). The cap evidence for the boundary: `1.40` (opus) capped once with the work done; `1.41` (sonnet) capped four times, three of them on re-verification of a finished branch — the 50-turn cap is spent on verification tails on both models, the reading #review-1-r1 made from the r1 chain.
- **Reversal.** This entry: `git revert` of its commit on `handoff/023`. Michael's decision: his to change, by a planning commit that supersedes `1.10.r5c` before the r5 set starts. The log renames: rename back.

## #028-correction-of-026-decision-2 (2026-09-12, principal, during the r5 chain; 1.43 attempt 1 withheld DONE on the clause #026 tried to widen; manual procedure, audited here; no planning commit)

- **What happened.** `1.43` attempt 1 (opus, 42 turns, exit 0) implemented all three deliverables — `toSource`'s no-spelling branch answers the end of the entered block's last preceding descendant (bounded to the block: the corpus showed an emptied table cell's cursor going to the previous row when the search ran over all entries), the width branch, the split collector and the `it.fails` case removed with both shapes as ordinary clause-3 assertions (the old answer restored went red on 14 fixtures), Sol's reproduction as `e2e/web/editor-toggle-trailing-break.spec.ts`; `pnpm lint`, `pnpm test` 38 files / 3,055 passed / **1 expected fail**, `cargo test`, e2e/web 86/86 — and withheld DONE, correctly, on the acceptance clause "`pnpm test`'s summary shows no expected fail": the one remaining expected fail is 1.40's pin in `packages/core/test/positions-corpus.test.ts` (the `formatWithMap` limitation, #026), which the task text's "No other source change" forbids it to fix. It recorded the two resolutions and stopped.
- **The correction.** #026's Decision 2 widened 1.43's scope to `packages/core/src/positions.ts` through a `[1.40]` lesson line, on the model of Decision 1 (which 1.40's agent accepted, its conflict being inside its own acceptance). A lesson line is orientation, not scope: CLAUDE.md says the scope is the task text, `ralph/tasks.json` is immutable to the agent, and the only instruments that change a task's text are a planning commit and a reconciliation. 1.43's agent read the widening as "a different task" and was right to. Decision 2 is withdrawn. **1.43 prints DONE on the branch as it stands** — the clause "no expected fail" is read, for 1.43, as the pin this task removes (its own `grep -c 'it.fails'` = 0 on `position-map-editor-leg.test.ts`), the acceptance's cross-check having been written at the r4 reconciliation when 1.37's pin was the only one in the tree and before 1.40 added its own. The `formatWithMap` fix (a text node whose edge character `containerPhrasing` encoded is placed; `placeChildren` looks the emission up by its encoded form when the literal misses; the pin folded into the ordinary case) is a **task for the next planning-capable commit**: appended by the `1.10.r5d` reconciliation to the r6 chain if the r5 verdict is FAIL (the reviewers will see the pin and its `[1.40, found outside scope]` line), otherwise carried into Phase 2 planning as the backlog line says, with the pin in the tree at close as #024 allows for an expected failure that goes red when fixed. Not a planning commit now: the graph does not change mid-chain for one task's scope, and a planning commit has no runner path outside a gate's plan request.
- **Procedure for 1.43.** The runner started attempt 2 on the withheld DONE and will run attempt 3 the same way; at `STUCK 1.43` (or earlier if an attempt prints DONE): the three logs moved aside with an attempt suffix, a `chore(1.43): principal lesson` commit on `task/1.43` stating this correction and the one remaining step (append its own journal line, run the suite once, print DONE; change nothing else), `ralph.sh retry 1.43`, restart. For the record the pattern this chain established: every task of the r5 chain so far (1.40, 1.41, 1.42, 1.43) finished its work in the first or second attempt and lost attempts to the verification tail or to a procedure rule; the `retry`-with-a-lesson shape recovered each.
- **Lesson (principal).** A reconciliation's acceptance sentence that asserts a suite-wide absence ("no expected fail") is false the moment a sibling task pins something; an acceptance names the file-level grep it owns and never the suite's total — 1.43's own lesson line says the same. And a mid-chain scope conflict is decided by reading which sentence yields (Decision 1's shape), never by widening a scope the instrument cannot reach.
- **Reversal.** This entry: `git revert` of its commit on `handoff/023`. #026's Decision 2: withdrawn here, not edited there (the record stands with this correction after it).

- **The cause, read in the installed package, and the shape 1.45 takes (this entry decides handoff 024's first open question).** `mdast-util-to-markdown/lib/util/container-phrasing.js` (2.1.2) rewrites a child's output after its handler returned at exactly two places: the first UTF-16 unit of the child after an attention run (`encodeAfter === value.slice(0, 1)` → `encodeCharacterReference(encodeAfter.charCodeAt(0)) + value.slice(1)`) and the last unit of the child before it (`results[last].slice(0, -1) + encodeCharacterReference(before.charCodeAt(0))`); `after` is the first unit of the next child's output (a speculative `handle`/`peek` call, `.charAt(0)`) and `before` the last unit of the previous. The **decision** to encode is right in the parser's own terms: micromark classifies one UTF-16 unit too (`micromark-util-classify-character` tests `String.fromCharCode(code)` against `\p{P}|\p{S}`, and a lone surrogate is neither whitespace nor punctuation), so a raw astral neighbour never lets the run form — `parse("~~a.~~😀\n")`, `parse("😀~~(a)~~\n")` and `parse("*a.*😀\n")` hold no mark, reproduced here — while the code-point reference does (`parse("~~a.~~&#x1F600;\n")` holds one `delete` with the neighbour text `😀`). Only the **width** of the encoding is wrong: one unit of a pair. Consequence: classification stays by code unit, matching the parser — a `codePointAt` classification would call 😀 punctuation (`\p{So}`), decide not to encode, and write bytes the parser cannot close, so Claude's proposal (i) is **not** taken as written and 1.45's text says so — and the encoding widens to the whole scalar. Prototyped here and taken: `state.containerPhrasing` wrapped once per serialization on the first dispatched node (always `root`; the `patchIndentLines` shape `positions.ts` already uses), so that each lone-surrogate reference the original wrote at a child boundary — `&#xD83D;` followed by the raw U+DE00, or the raw U+D83D followed by `&#xDE00;`, the only two forms the two rewrites can produce, `safe()` and the handlers' inside-whitespace encoding never encoding a surrogate — becomes the code-point reference of the pair: with the prototype, 96 of 96 trees (three marks × {😀, 𐐀, 𝕒, `b`} × {punctuation, whitespace, inline code, link, letter} inside × {closing, opening, both sides}) are fixed points holding one mark whose parsed text equals the tree's, and the five invariant-A sources above format to themselves; the app's `root` handler mirrors `lib/handle/root.js`'s four lines with `mdast-util-phrasing`'s twelve-type list because neither package is a direct dependency of `packages/core` and a manifest line is a lockfile change. This is Sol's shape (the boundary encoding consumes and emits a whole scalar on both sides) with the decision left where it is: the built-in `emphasis`/`strong` handlers untouched, `handleDelete` unchanged, `formatWithMap` inheriting the wrapper through `createFormatter()`. Checked before writing the tasks: the scratch suite run with the prototype and a fixture `astral-neighbour.md` in the index (3,089 tests) left five reds, all the fixture's — the position-map deletion leg puts an encoded neighbour last in a block (K2's own class, green only after 1.46), `typeInsideEveryBlock` inserted its letter between the two units of a surrogate pair (a leg that cuts by code unit), and the letter leg's "delete the letters, get the canonical back" model cannot predict a letter that lands flush against a mark and is encoded — so the fixture enters the index at **1.47**, after the map fix, with the leg's code-point rule and the fixture's second paragraph shaped `x &#x10400;*(a)*` (the model limit filed on the backlog); a first run of the prototype had listed `html` among the phrasing types and turned raw-html.md red — upstream calls html ambiguous and excludes it, and 1.45's text says so. → **1.45** (opus; `packages/core/src/format.ts`, a file of passed Phase 0 tasks and of 1.40 — a new task, #016).

## #029-gate-1.verify.r6h-transient-rerun-second-instance-of-023 (2026-09-13, principal; manual procedure, audited here; no planning commit)

- **What happened.** `scripts/gate.sh 1.verify.r6h` a1 (ci.yml run 34735711543 at `c2f6526`, ref `ci/1.verify.r6/a1`, Michael's shell): macos-latest 88 Playwright cases passed, ubuntu-latest 88 passed with the runner conformance suite green, cargo green on all three; windows-latest 87 passed, 1 failed: `e2e/web/editor-soft-line-breaks.spec.ts:111` › "G4: a space typed at the end of the first line of a wrapped paragraph carries no entity" — the location assertion at line 133 polled 5,000 ms and read the caret at `offset: 6` (the start of `beta gamma`) where 11 `ArrowLeft` presses from the asserted anchor (offset 16) should leave it at 5; one press short, before the space was typed, so no bytes were produced and no entity was asserted. `GATE-FAILED`, `1.verify.r6h` → `blocked`, plan request `plan.1.verify.r6h.r0` (`03:39:05Z`).
- **Transient, not code-caused (the #023 criterion).** The spec last changed at `task(1.39)` `af8a62c`, before the r5h gate; the same case passed on windows-latest at `1.verify.r5h` a1 (run 34705334786 at `3dd9c4b`, 735 ms). The tree between `3dd9c4b` and `c2f6526` changes, outside tests and docs, only `packages/core/src/format.ts` (1.45) and `packages/core/src/positions.ts` (1.46), plus two new spec files; nothing in `packages/editor/src` or `apps/`, so the rendered view's DOM and Blink's caret motion are the ones r5h exercised, and the case's seeded text (`alpha\nbeta gamma`) is ASCII the 1.45 encoding cannot reach. The anchor assertion passed (the 1.46 map carried the caret to the block's end), so the lost press is between two Blink motions in an unchanged view. A `.g1` repair would have had nothing to change; RUNNER-SPEC §2 gives the rerun for exactly this.
- **The rerun.** `scripts/gate.sh rerun 1.verify.r6h` (Michael's shell, `04:14:50Z`): the plan request withdrawn by the rerun (`abandoned`, reason suffixed "withdrawn: transient rerun"), a2 (run 34737435949, same SHA `c2f6526`, ref `ci/1.verify.r6/a2`) 88/88/88 on the three runners (the G4 case 1.2 s on windows), `ACCEPT` at `04:22:24Z`, `accepted.json` → a2, both artifacts fetched under `a2/`.
- **The runner contradicting itself, again as #023.** After the ACCEPT `ralph/ralph.sh doctor` reported `incomplete 1.verify.r6h a1 (workflow failed, state not transitioned) → scripts/gate.sh 1.verify.r6h --resume a1` — the same missing doctor clause (`ralph/lib/doctor.mjs`: a `rejected` attempt on a `passed` gate) and the same wrong remedy (`--resume a1` would overwrite the a2 `passed` record). No `fix(runner)` now for #023's reason: the fix is a commit on `phase/1` before the r6 reviewers start, and `stepReview` refuses on any head other than `c2f6526`.
- **Manual procedure (this gate, once; #023's, run by the principal at `04:23:12Z`).** `scripts/gate.sh abandon 1.verify.r6h a1 --reason "rejected a1 superseded by the transient rerun a2 (ACCEPT); marked abandoned only so doctor stops reading it as incomplete (DECISIONS #023 procedure, recorded as #029)"` — writes `a1/abandoned.json` and an audit line, changes no task state (the gate is `passed`); `doctor` clean afterwards. The word is a misrecord as in #023: a1 was rejected and superseded; the audit log and this entry are the truth.
- **For the r6 reconciliation.** `.evidence/ci/1.verify.r6h/` holds a1 (rejected, `abandoned.json` by the procedure) and a2 (accepted); `verification_sha == implementation_sha == c2f6526` unchanged; `plan.1.verify.r6h.r0` reads `abandoned` — by the rerun, not by `plan-abandon`; no graph change, `EXPECTED_COUNT` stays 232. Second instance of #023's contradiction: the boundary `fix(runner)` #023 scopes (the doctor clause, the refused `--resume` on a superseded attempt, one conformance test per guard, `doctor` clean at the end of the transient-rerun test) is unchanged in kind and now has two instances. This entry is on `handoff/025` (stacked; cherry-picked by `1.10.r6d`, rotate.md step 5), which is why it carries no journal line.
- **Reversal.** The abandon: `rm .evidence/ci/1.verify.r6h/a1/abandoned.json` returns doctor to its finding (the audit line stays). The rerun: none — a2 is an accepted attempt with remote side effects (§2: never rolled back). This entry: `git revert` of its commit.

Full suite and every e2e/web spec green on three OSes at the implementation SHA, digests matched: pass (3 of 3; at a2, the a1 rerun recorded above and dispositioned as L7). ## #035-gate-1.verify.r7h-windows-spec-defects (2026-09-21, plan.1.verify.r7h.r0; principal, from the run's log; scope per DECISIONS #019, #021, #022, #024, handoff 028)

- **What happened.** `scripts/gate.sh 1.verify.r7h` a1 (ci.yml run 35629907462 at `fd03e40`, Michael's shell): every unit test file, `cargo test` and the lint step green on all three runners; ubuntu-latest and macos-latest 93/93 Playwright cases; windows-latest **91 passed, 2 failed**: (1) `e2e/web/editor-astral-between-runs.spec.ts:157` (task 1.49) — `page.evaluate: TypeError: Failed to fetch dynamically imported module: http://localhost:1420/@fsD:/a/essaydown/essaydown/packages/core/src/index.ts` at line 144, `formatParseInPage`; (2) `e2e/web/editor-soft-line-breaks.spec.ts:126` G4 — task 1.54's per-step poll in `press` timed out at line 86 during the `ArrowLeft` count at line 145: one press never moved the DOM caret. `GATE-FAILED`, `1.verify.r7h` → `blocked`, plan request `plan.1.verify.r7h.r0`. Artifacts fetched only on success (#020); the gate's `workflow.log` is the evidence. Both are spec defects; the product is unchanged since `e28c273` and no source file is in the repair's scope.
- **Cause (1), read.** 1.49's task text asked for the fixed point "computed in the page"; the agent imported the core module through Vite's `/@fs` route with a `fileURLToPath` path — a Node path, which on Windows carries a drive letter and no leading slash, so the URL lacks the slash Vite's route needs. The sibling `editor-astral-neighbour.spec.ts` (1.45) asserts the same fixed point in the test process (`expect(format(parse(pane))).toBe(pane)`), green on all three runners since r6h; the reconciliation's phrase was the error (lesson below). The spec's first three-OS run is this gate, so the defect could not have shown earlier.
- **Cause (2), read — two readings, the fix task decides neither.** The step that never moves the caret is the last of eleven `ArrowLeft` from the block's end: the crossing of the soft line break from offset 6 (the start of `beta gamma`) to offset 5 (after `alpha`, before the LF). 1.verify.r6h a1 (#029) read offset 6 after the blind count — the same step — and Sol's r6 review reproduced it once on Linux under load; a2 passed. With 1.54's poll the lost step is named instead of inferred. Reading (i): Blink on that runner drops the leftward crossing of a preserved LF under `white-space: pre-wrap`. Reading (ii): Blink there reports the caret at the first line's end as offset 6 (the two DOM spellings of one visual position), so 5 is unreachable by any motion. The container is Linux and cannot tell them apart; a route right under both is chosen, and the gate's received value at the new rights-count assertion discriminates (a 5 confirms (i); a 6 confirms (ii), and the next planning commit adopts the tree discriminator alone).
- **Scope decision (explicit, so the fix task does not pick).** (A) The `/@fs` import replaced by the sibling's test-process `format(parse(·))` — the imports already exist in the file; `fileURLToPath` and `CORE_INDEX` deleted; nothing else in the spec changes. (B) G4's count replaced by a route that never crosses the break leftwards: ten `ArrowLeft` asserted at offset 6, one `ArrowUp` asserted at offset 0 (from the line's left edge, an x-independent landing, followed by its assertion per #024), five `ArrowRight` asserted at offset 5; then the space, and the discriminator the bytes cannot give (#022: assert the tree): the text node reads `alpha \nbeta gamma`. The `press` helper (1.54's two forms) is unchanged: it did its job. Not chosen: a rerun (#023's criterion needs an unchanged spec and this gate ran twelve rewritten helpers and four new specs for the first time on windows; and (1) is deterministic); a click at a computed point (#024's reason); relaxing the offset-5 precondition (the location assertion is the case's only discriminating power until the tree assertion is in).
- **Graph change (RUNNER-SPEC §2 rule (a)), both physically in PRD §8 and `ralph/tasks.json`.** Appended after `1.verify.r7`: **`1.55`** (opus; the two spec files only; depends on `1.verify.r7h`) and **`1.verify.r7.g1`** (sonnet; `needsCI`, `refTemplate ci/1.verify.r7.g1/a{n}`, `artifactNames` unchanged; depends on `1.55`; the promote-lessons step of `1.verify.r7` is integrated at `fd03e40` and is not repeated; `ciAcceptance` names `/logs/ci/1.verify.r7.g1h/accepted/`). The r7 reviewers are explicit physical tasks (#review-1-r6): `1.10.r7a/b/c` now depend on `1.verify.r7.g1` (the generator's expansion makes that `1.verify.r7.g1h`) and the `/logs/ci/1.verify.r7h/accepted/` reference in each description reads `/logs/ci/1.verify.r7.g1h/accepted/`; their texts are otherwise unchanged. `ralph/EXPECTED_COUNT` 244 → 247 (196 raw + 21 review-set + 30 CI gates). `1.verify.r7h` becomes `superseded` at `sync-state`.
- **Model choice.** `1.55` is opus for #024's reason: the container cannot observe the failing runner, a miss costs a CI round and another planning commit, and (B) asks for a route argued for a geometry the task cannot see. `1.verify.r7.g1` is sonnet, as every verifier.
- **Acceptance tails, this time.** `1.55`'s acceptance names file-level greps and the two spec files; it asserts nothing about the suite's total beyond "green" (#032's lesson).
- **For the reconciliation `1.10.r7d`.** DECISIONS #030–#034 live on `handoff/027`/`handoff/028` and this entry lands in the tree first, so `git cherry-pick phase/1..handoff/028` will conflict at the tail of `docs/DECISIONS.md` (two appends at the same place): resolve by keeping both, #030–#034 after #035 (the entries carry their dates; the numeric order records when each reached the tree). The r7 reviewers will read #035 in the tree and #030–#034 through the named ref.
- **Reversal.** Before this planning commit integrates: `git -C .wt/plan.1.verify.r7h.r0 reset --hard phase/1` (the branch back to its cut at `fd03e40`); the request stays `running`, the status `ralph.sh plan` left it in — `plan-retry` accepts only `blocked`/`integration-failed` and `plan-abandon` moves it to `abandoned`, so no ralph command returns it to `pending`; the reversal is git-only, as in #018–#024. After integration a plan request has no undo: `1.verify.r7h` stays `superseded` and any further change is another planning commit.

## #036-gate-1.verify.r7.g1h-g4-native-motion-reverted (2026-09-22, plan.1.verify.r7.g1h.r0; principal, from the run's log; scope per DECISIONS #019, #021, #022, #024, #035, handoff 028)

- **What happened.** `scripts/gate.sh 1.verify.r7.g1h` a1 (ci.yml run 35685069545 at `5a39abd`, Michael's shell): every unit test file, `cargo test` and the lint step green on all three runners; ubuntu-latest and macos-latest 93/93 Playwright cases; windows-latest **92 passed, 1 failed**: `e2e/web/editor-soft-line-breaks.spec.ts:131` G4, the assertion at line 164 after task 1.55's route — expected offset 5 after five `ArrowRight` from an asserted 0, **received 11**; the ten-left assertion (offset 6) and the `ArrowUp` assertion (offset 0) had both passed. 1.49's `/@fs` repair held (the astral spec green on all three). `GATE-FAILED`, `1.verify.r7.g1h` → `blocked`, plan request `plan.1.verify.r7.g1h.r0`. Artifacts fetched only on success (#020); `workflow.log` is the evidence.
- **Cause, read.** 11 = 6 + 5: the caret was back at offset 6 when the rights began, after the DOM had reported 0. Neither of #035's readings: not a dropped step and not a second spelling of the same position, but a native motion **reverted** by ProseMirror before the next key — PM learns a native caret move from `selectionchange`, dispatched in the rendering update after the key, and writes its own (stale) selection back to the DOM on the next key's handling when it has not yet seen the move (the `[1.46, found outside scope]` class, recorded at r6 for the toggle chord after an arrow; the 200 ms tick in two specs is its named workaround). The container and the other two runners are past the rendering step before the next synthetic key; windows-latest is not, reliably at this one place — three gates, three routes (#029's blind count, #035's polled count, this up-then-rights), the same visual position. The r6h a1 and r7h a1 "lost press" were the same revert one step earlier (the DOM moved 6 → 5, PM wrote 6 back inside the poll's sampling interval).
- **Scope decision (explicit, so the fix task does not pick).** Candidates: (1) another arrow route — every one has a second key after a native motion, the exact race; (2) a wait after each arrow (the 200 ms tick) — a timing sleep in the gate's own instrument, and the backlog line already calls it a workaround; (3) **a click at a computed point** — the route CLAUDE.md and #024 allow beside arrows, one native event with no second key in flight, after which PM syncs its selection from the DOM on `mouseup`; the point is the right edge of the last `a` of `alpha` measured from the text node's own `Range` rect, so it is measured geometry, not assumed metrics; (4) relaxing the precondition — since 1.55 the case's proof is the text node after the keystroke (`alpha \nbeta gamma`), which is exact whichever route places the caret. Chosen: (3) with (4) kept: the click places, the recorded offset is asserted as the container reports it, and the discriminator proves where the space went. `1.56` is sonnet: the route is specified, the container shows it green or not, and the only unobservable is the runner — #024's reason for opus was an unspecified route.
- **Graph change (RUNNER-SPEC §2 rule (a)), both physically in PRD §8 and `ralph/tasks.json`.** Appended after `1.verify.r7.g1`: **`1.56`** (sonnet; that spec file, one backlog evidence line; depends on `1.verify.r7.g1h`) and **`1.verify.r7.g2`** (sonnet; `needsCI`, `refTemplate ci/1.verify.r7.g2/a{n}`, `artifactNames` unchanged; depends on `1.56`; `ciAcceptance` names `/logs/ci/1.verify.r7.g2h/accepted/`). `1.10.r7a/b/c` now depend on `1.verify.r7.g2` (the generator makes that `1.verify.r7.g2h`) and their `/logs/ci` reference reads `1.verify.r7.g2h`; otherwise unchanged. `ralph/EXPECTED_COUNT` 247 → 250 (198 raw + 21 review-set + 31 CI gates). `1.verify.r7.g1h` becomes `superseded` at `sync-state`.
- **For the record.** This is the fourth CI round on one case (r6h a1 → a2, r7h, r7.g1h). The product path is unchanged since `e28c273`; the cost is the gate instrument on one runner. The product-side question — whether a real user can hit the revert — stays on the `[1.46]` line (a human does not press two keys inside one frame); Phase 2's toggle task settles the DOM selection before reading it.
- **Reversal.** Before this planning commit integrates: `git -C .wt/plan.1.verify.r7.g1h.r0 reset --hard phase/1`; the request stays `running` (git-only, as #018–#024, #035). After integration a plan request has no undo.

## #030-task-1.51-inline-html-fixture-and-trailing-atom-inverse (2026-09-21, principal, during the r7 chain; STUCK 1.51 after three attempts on a task-text conflict the agent correctly refused to decide; manual procedure, audited here; no planning commit — the graph is unchanged)

- **What happened.** `1.51` attempt 1 (opus, `error_max_turns` at 51, stub present, no stop signal) built the map fix — `rewrittenEmissions`'s fourth candidate for the eol-before-html branch and the `inlineCode` spelling table in `packages/core/src/positions.ts` —, the fixture `inline-html.md` as the task text specified it, the unit guards in a new `packages/core/test/positions-html-eol-inline-code.test.ts`, the toggle inverse cases in `toggle.test.ts` and most of `e2e/web/editor-toggle-inline-html.spec.ts`. Attempt 2 (43 turns, exit 0) finished the spec, widened `positions.test.ts`'s "one table per text node and none for any other node" case to `inlineCode` (the task's own scope, a change its deliverable requires), ran lint and the suites — `pnpm test` 3,528 passed / **8 failed** — and withheld DONE, correctly, writing two conflicts to its journal line (`Status: Blocked`) and two lessons, deciding neither (CLAUDE.md: a task that conflicts with the rules stops and does not decide). Attempt 3 (9 turns) re-confirmed the same reds and stopped. `STUCK 1.51` (`blocked`, attempts 3). The eight reds, from the transcript: `roundtrip.test.ts` › `inline-html.md` › B semantic preservation; `editor-fixed-point.test.ts` › `inline-html.md` after a letter is typed inside every block, after a period is typed at the end of every marked run, after the whitespace beside every marked run is deleted; `position-map-editor-leg.test.ts` › `inline-html.md` after a letter is typed inside every block, and its "ran both legs over every fixture" reach case; `toggle.test.ts` › the two new inverse cases on `alpha\n<i>beta</i>` and `*a*\n<b>x</b>`.
- **Conflict 1, read.** The fixture as specified — paragraphs holding a soft line break before inline html — reaches the one serializer rewrite that changes a *text value*: `container-phrasing.js` lines 60–72 replace the previous child's trailing line ending with a space before an `html` child, so `parse(format(parse(source)))` holds `alpha beta ` where the source held `alpha beta\n`. Six instruments outside the task's scope encode "the serializer never rewrites a text value", true of every fixture until this one: invariant B's position-stripped tree equality of source and canonical, the editor-fixed-point letter leg's `out.split(letter).join("") === canonical` model, the two mark's-neighbour legs' text equality (the K4 instrument), and the position-map editor leg's letter clause with its reach case. The task text asked for both "the fixture … byte-identical on a second pass" and "the … positions and editor-fixed-point suites green with their meaning unchanged", with those files outside its scope: the two sentences cannot hold together. The error is the reconciliation's: it wrote the fixture without reading invariant B for the value change — lesson [1.40]'s class one rewrite later, as the agent's own lesson says.
- **Decision 1 (the fixture yields; no suite outside the task's scope changes).** `inline-html.md` enters the index holding inline html **without** a soft line break before it: three paragraphs — `alpha <i>beta</i> gamma` (mid-paragraph), `*a* <b>x</b> y` (beside a mark), `<span>x</span> gamma` (leading) — source and canonical byte-identical, so the identity corpus, every corpus leg and "maps every node of the tree, and nothing is unresolved" cover the placement of an inline `html` atom beside text and marks. The third-branch member (a soft break before html) is guarded by the task's own unit guards (`alpha beta\n<span>x</span> gamma`, `alpha\n<i>beta</i>`, `*a*\n<b>x</b>`: `unresolved` empty, the line ending spelled as the space, `nodeAt`, the mutation red with the fourth candidate removed) and by the Playwright route (`alpha\n<i>beta</i>` loaded, toggle, `X`, `alphaX <i>beta</i>\n`), which exercises the real rewrite end to end. The promoted rule (b) — the corpus case that says nothing is unresolved is the guard that names which rewrites exist — is met for the two edge encodings and the wrapper's three forms by `strikethrough-punctuation.md`, `astral-neighbour.md` and `astral-between-runs.md`; for the third branch it is met by the unit guard and its mutation, because the only source that fires that branch fails invariant B by upstream's construction. Recorded here so the r7 reviewers read it as decided, not as an omission. Whether the app keeps upstream's rewrite at all — a user's soft break before inline html becomes a space on save; the rewrite is over-broad (a paragraph continuation line cannot start an html block of type 7, CommonMark §4.6) but safe — is a product decision for Phase 2, with real files: **backlog line** — the first Phase 2 task that loads a file from disk decides; if the rewrite is kept, invariant B's comparison names the exception (a text node's trailing line ending before an `html` sibling reads as a space) and the soft-break fixture enters the index then; if it is pre-empted (a `containerPhrasing` wrapper that keeps the line ending when the html child cannot start a block), the fixture enters byte-identical. Hard stop `2.verify`.
- **Conflict 2, read.** The two inverse cases fail at exactly one position each: the block end after a trailing inline atom (`html`, ProseMirror size 1, no spelling table), where `cursorMap` answers the node's start; the no-break control `alpha <i>beta</i>` fails there too, so it is not the L3 defect. It is L5's class — Claude finding 4 names the cause, `delimiterPosition` (`toggle.ts:441`) returning `entry.pmStart` for anything that is not a mark — which the task text places in 1.52 and forbids 1.51 to touch ("No change to toggle.ts").
- **Decision 2 (the inverse cases name the exclusion; the member is 1.52's).** The two html inverse cases assert every text position except the one block-end position after a trailing inline atom, named in the case with this entry and 1.52 as the owner; the no-break control the same. The trailing-atom block end is recorded here as a **member of L5**: the same branch of `delimiterPosition`, in the function 1.52 edits, so 1.52's rule ("at or past a node's end on that line, answer the end") covers a cell's end and a block ending in an inline atom alike, and 1.52's "members named here" includes this one — its named guards add one document ending in an html atom; its corpus leg meets no such fixture (the Decision-1 fixture ends no paragraph in an atom). This adds a member to a family the neighbouring task already owns, in the file and function its text names; it is not #028's widening (a different file and task).
- **Acceptance of 1.51, re-read clause by clause.** "The fixture present with its canonical sibling and index entry, byte-identical through every identity leg and every corpus leg and resolved by 'maps every node…'": holds with the Decision-1 fixture. "The three html guards and the inline-code guards green": the unit guards, unchanged. "The toggle inverse cases green with the position set computed from the doc": every position but the one named exclusion. "The cursor, toggle, 200-seeded-position, positions and editor-fixed-point suites green with their meaning unchanged": holds — the one in-file change (`positions.test.ts`'s table-per-node case now expects a table for `inlineCode` too) is the deliverable's own consequence inside the task's scope; the journal names it. The Playwright clause, the doc-comment clauses and the `git diff --stat` clause unchanged.
- **Instrument.** This entry on `handoff/027` (cut from `phase/1` at `faf6103`, after handoff 026 entered the tree at `695fb0f` — rotate.md step 5's third case; cherry-picked by `1.10.r7d`), and one `chore(1.51): principal lesson` commit on `task/1.51` in `.wt/1.51` carrying the two decisions as `[1.51]` lines in `docs/lessons.md` (the #026/#028 shape; `docs/lessons.md` only). The retry: FIRST append a NEW `- [1.51] ` journal line with one `printf` that prints the timestamp and commit it as the stub; reshape the fixture per Decision 1 (source, canonical, index entry, and every reach assertion of the task's own tests that names the fixture); rewrite the two inverse cases and the control per Decision 2; change nothing else on the branch (the map fix, the guards, the spec and the `positions.test.ts` widening stand); run the suite ONCE (`pnpm lint && pnpm test && cargo test`, then the e2e/web suite); complete the stub in place, naming the eight reds of attempt 2 and the clause each decision answers; commit; print DONE. The three logs moved aside as `.evidence/tasks/1.51/{1.a1-capped,2.a2-blocked,3.a3-blocked}.log` (#027). `ralph/ralph.sh retry 1.51` (`blocked → pending`, attempts 0, `no_commit_streak` 0; the branch and worktree are kept), then the restart.
- **Why not a planning commit.** The graph is unchanged; the conflict is between one task's sentences and is resolved by choosing which yields (#026's shape under #028's rule): Decision 1 narrows the fixture inside the task's own scope; Decision 2 names a member of a family the next task already owns.
- **Lesson (principal; PRINCIPAL.md at the boundary, with #026's and #028's).** A reconciliation that writes a fixture for a serializer branch that rewrites a value reads invariant B and every index-walking editor leg for that value change before writing the task text, and names the assertion that yields or keeps the shape out of the index. A task asking for an every-position inverse over a document that ends in a node class without a spelling table probes the control's last position first; if it fails there the failure is the neighbouring task's, written as a named exclusion in the task text. Both are the 1.51 agent's own lessons (`docs/lessons.md`, `[1.51]` 08:27:46Z), adopted.
- **Reversal.** This entry: `git revert` of its commit on `handoff/027` (or `git branch -D handoff/027` before it is cherry-picked). The lesson commit: `git -C .wt/1.51 reset --hard HEAD~1` before the retry's first attempt commits. The log renames: rename back. The retry: none once an attempt has run (an attempt is consumed); before it, no `ralph.sh` command returns `pending` to `blocked` — the state file is not hand-edited, so the reversal of `retry` is to let the task run.

## #031-correction-of-030-decision-1 (2026-09-21, principal, during the r7 chain; the 1.51 retry's attempt 1 stopped, correctly, on the #030 fixture bytes; manual procedure, audited here; no planning commit)

- **What happened.** The retry's attempt 1 (opus, 37 turns, exit 0) verified #030 on `handoff/027`, implemented Decision 2 exactly (a `trailingAtomBlockEnds(doc)` helper computed from the doc, the exclusion asserted to be exactly one position on exactly the two html sources, all 12 inverse cases green) and wrote Decision 1's three paragraphs to both fixture files; `pnpm lint` green, `pnpm test` 41 files, 3,532 passed, **4 failed**, all four in `packages/editor/test/position-map-editor-leg.test.ts` on the fixture's second paragraph `*a* <b>x</b> y`, and neither red is about html: (a) the letter leg types after the block's first character — the one-character run `a` inside `*a*` — so the letter lands at the mark's end (`*aQ*`, the mark inclusive) and `cursorMap.toSource` hands the boundary to the later node (`innermostAt`), answering the column after the closing `*`, where the leg's model expects the letter at `ch - 1`; `*a* y` alone fails the same way; (b) the deletion leg deletes `y`, the conversion strips the space after `</b>`, the block now ends in the html atom, and the leg's clause-3 oracle `byteEndOf` answers a non-text last leaf with its *start* (ch 8) where `lastNodeEnd` answers its end (ch 12) — the trailing-atom block end of #030 Decision 2, met from the deletion side. The two reach cases go red only because (a) and (b) never pushed the fixture's name. The agent probed, restored and did not commit a variant with two-character runs — `alpha <i>beta</i> gamma`, `*ab* <b>x</b> yz`, `<span>x</span> gamma` — under which the whole unit suite is green (41 files, 3,536 passed, 0 failed), wrote a backlog line naming both models with triggers, and stopped without DONE for the principal (CLAUDE.md: stop, write it, do not decide). `cargo test` and the e2e/web suite were not reached. The error is again the reconciliation's, now #030's: it chose the fixture's bytes without running the index-walking legs over them, having just recorded the lesson that says to.
- **Decision 1, amended (the fixture's bytes; nothing else changes).** `inline-html.md` and its canonical sibling hold exactly the agent's green variant: `alpha <i>beta</i> gamma`, `*ab* <b>x</b> yz`, `<span>x</span> gamma` — two-character runs at the mark and at the block end, so the letter leg's first insertion is not at a mark's edge and the deletion leg's one-character deletion leaves text after the atom. The index entry is unchanged (`nodeTypes` emphasis/html/paragraph/root/text, `blockCount` 3, `paragraphStartLines` 1/3/5, as attempt 1 checked). This is the `[1.47, letter leg's model]` precedent (1.47's second paragraph starts with `x ` for the same leg), inside 1.51's own scope; widening the two leg models is outside it and is not taken here. Option (i) of the agent's two.
- **The two models, owned.** (a) is a member of **L6** (#review-1-r6, task 1.53): with the boundary side decided by `doc.resolve(pos).marks()`, a caret at the end of an inclusive run toggles to inside the closing delimiter, and the letter leg's `ch - 1` model holds at a mark's end without a fixture-aware skip — 1.53's guards include a one-character marked first run (`*a* y`, the letter typed after `a`) asserting the leg's model, and after 1.53 a fixture may hold that shape. (b) is a member of **L5** (task 1.52, #030 Decision 2, from the deletion side): 1.52's rule sides with `lastNodeEnd`, so `byteEndOf`'s non-text branch in `position-map-editor-leg.test.ts` — in 1.52's scope, `packages/editor/test/**` — answers a block whose reparsed last leaf is an inline atom with that leaf's end, with one named guard (a paragraph left ending in an html atom by a deletion). The agent's `[1.51, two position-map editor-leg models …]` backlog line stands as the record of both, with these owners in place of its `2.verify` revisit; 1.52's and 1.53's agents read this entry through the `[1.51]` lesson lines (the tasks' texts are immutable; each member lives in the file and function its task already owns, #028's rule kept).
- **Instrument.** This entry stacked on `handoff/027` (not yet in the tree; `1.10.r7d` cherry-picks `phase/1..handoff/027`). At `STUCK 1.51` (the runner runs attempts 2 and 3 of the retry the same way; or earlier if one prints DONE, which the decided bytes forbid): one `chore(1.51): principal lesson` commit on `task/1.51` (`docs/lessons.md` only) carrying the amended bytes and the remaining steps — FIRST a NEW `- [1.51] ` journal line by one `printf`, committed as the stub; write the three paragraphs to both fixture files (the index entry unchanged); change nothing else (Decision 2 as implemented on the branch stands); `e2e/web/node_modules/.bin/playwright install chromium` (lesson [1.50]); the suite ONCE (`pnpm lint && pnpm test && cargo test`, then e2e/web); complete the stub naming attempt 2's eight reds, the retry's four reds and the clause each decision answers, and the in-scope `positions.test.ts` meaning change; commit; DONE. The retry's three logs moved aside as `.evidence/tasks/1.51/{1.r1a1-blocked,2.r1a2-blocked,3.r1a3-blocked}.log` (#027). `ralph/ralph.sh retry 1.51` (`blocked → pending`, attempts 0), the restart, a fresh watcher (handoff 027's gotcha).
- **Lesson (principal; PRINCIPAL.md at the boundary with #030's).** A decision that fixes a fixture's bytes runs the index-walking legs over those bytes in a scratch worktree before it is written — the same rule #030 recorded for the reconciliation, applied to the decision that corrected it; two attempts of a task were spent on a check that takes one minute on the host.
- **Reversal.** This entry: `git revert` of its commit on `handoff/027`. The lesson commit: `git -C .wt/1.51 reset --hard HEAD~1` before the second retry's attempt commits. The log renames: rename back. The retry: none once an attempt has run; before it, let the task run.

## #032-task-1.52-code-block-interior-and-the-no-expected-fail-clause (2026-09-21, principal, during the r7 chain; written while 1.52's attempt 2 runs, from attempt 1's branch and transcript; manual procedure, audited here; no planning commit)

- **What happened.** `1.52` attempt 1 (opus, `error_max_turns` at 51, stub present) built the task: `containerEnd` and `isLeafEnd` in `packages/editor/src/toggle.ts` (the cell's end and the trailing leaf, #review-1-r6 L5 with #030 Decision 2's member), the corpus inverse leg and its destructive-transaction leg in a new `packages/editor/test/position-map-inverse.test.ts`, `byteEndOf`'s leaf end in `position-map-editor-leg.test.ts` (#031 (b)), 1.51's named exclusion lifted in `toggle.test.ts`, both Playwright routes green, two mutations recorded. The corpus leg found one class outside the task's named members: **every caret position inside a fenced code block** maps to the block's start and back to the position before the block (`code-fence-basic.md` 13 interior positions, `code-fence-nested.md` 91, `essay-fixture.md` 970) — the map's third clause by design (task 1.7): a leaf block is placed, never entered, because `formatWithMap` builds a spelling table for `text` and, since 1.51, `inlineCode` only, and a `code` node's bytes are not its value (fence line, info string, container prefixes). The task text's rule for such a class — a backlog line, and "the leg asserts it as a named expected member (`it.fails` with the fixture named) rather than being narrowed — stop and write the conflict to the journal if that would leave `pnpm test` with an expected fail (DECISIONS #028)" — contradicts itself for any member that is a real defect: the attempt wrote the backlog line `[1.52, code block interior]`, an ordinary case pinning that every non-identity position on those three fixtures is inside a `code_block` and nothing else, and three `it.fails` members; `pnpm test` then shows **3 expected fail**, which the same sentence says to stop on, and which the acceptance tails of 1.53, 1.54 and `1.verify.r7` ("`pnpm test`'s summary shows no expected fail") would each fail on — #028's own lesson ("an acceptance names the file-level grep it owns and never the suite's total"), broken by the reconciliation that cited it. The attempt was cut before reaching that stop. Attempt 2 is running as this is written; it will meet the sentence.
- **Decision (which sentence yields).** The class is a member of the cursor map's "answers some start" family (J3 → K2 → L4; L4's value-bearing *inline* node has its table since 1.51, the value-bearing *block* node does not) and its fix is a spelling table for `code` in `positions.ts` — 1.51's file, not 1.52's, and a per-line table (the value's line `n` at the fence body's line `n` after the container prefix) that is its own task. **The `it.fails` form yields; the class is a named exclusion asserted positively, and the suite holds no expected fail.** In `position-map-inverse.test.ts`: (i) the three `it.fails` cases are removed; (ii) the ordinary case for the three member fixtures asserts, as it already does, that every non-identity position is inside a `code_block` and nothing else, **and** that at least one such position exists per member fixture — the assertion that goes red the day `code` gets its table, so the exclusion cannot outlive its cause (the #024 shape for an expected failure, written as an ordinary guard); (iii) the leg's title and file comment say "the inverse holds at every text position outside a fenced code block's interior" and name the backlog line; (iv) the two legs' reach assertions count the excluded positions so the exclusion is exactly the code-block interiors and nothing else. The `[1.52, code block interior]` backlog line stands with its trigger (the first task that gives `code` a spelling table in `positions.ts` removes the exclusion and the positive guard together) and hard stop `2.verify`; add to it that the class is L4's block-level sibling and Claude's r6 risk 2's remaining member, so the r7 reviewers read it as decided. Nothing else on the branch changes. The acceptance of 1.52 is then met literally: the corpus leg green over every fixture with its reach count, the named members green, "`pnpm test`'s summary shows no expected fail" true; 1.53's, 1.54's and `1.verify.r7`'s tails hold without a re-reading.
- **If 1.52 integrates before this entry reaches its agent** (an attempt prints DONE with the three `it.fails` in the tree): the three later tasks' "no expected fail" clause is read, for each, as #028 reads it — no expected fail in the files the task touches, the file-level grep — and the conversion above is the first sentence of the next planning-capable commit (`1.10.r7d`'s chain if the verdict is FAIL, else Phase 2 planning), with the three expected fails in the tree at close as #024 allows for an expected failure that goes red when fixed. This branch is written down so that no later agent has to decide it.
- **Instrument.** This entry stacked on `handoff/027`. If attempt 2 or 3 stops on the sentence → at `STUCK 1.52`: one `chore(1.52): principal lesson` commit on `task/1.52` (`docs/lessons.md` only) carrying the decision and the steps — FIRST a NEW `- [1.52] ` journal line by one `printf`; the conversion (i)–(iv) in `position-map-inverse.test.ts` and the backlog line's addendum; nothing else; the suite ONCE (`pnpm lint && pnpm test && cargo test`, `playwright install chromium`, then e2e/web); complete the stub naming the class, the conversion and every guard's mutation already recorded; commit; DONE — the logs moved aside as `.evidence/tasks/1.52/{1.a1-capped,2.a2-…,3.a3-…}.log` with the stop's word (#027), `ralph/ralph.sh retry 1.52` (`blocked → pending`, attempts 0), the restart, a fresh watcher. If an attempt prints DONE with the `it.fails` in place: no retry; the second paragraph applies and the lesson line goes on `task/1.53` when its worktree exists, before its first attempt only if the runner is idle — otherwise 1.53's agent reads this entry through `git show handoff/027:docs/DECISIONS.md` named in its own conflict record.
- **Lesson (principal; PRINCIPAL.md at the boundary with #028's).** An acceptance tail copied into every task of a chain must not assert a suite-wide absence: "no expected fail" is false the moment one task of the chain pins a class it does not own, and a rule that tells a task both to pin and to stop on the pin is a contradiction the reconciliation wrote, not the agent. The tail names the file-level grep each task owns; a corpus property leg written for the first time names its exclusions positively (present, bounded, red when fixed) and never as `it.fails`.
- **Reversal.** This entry: `git revert` of its commit on `handoff/027`. The lesson commit: `git -C .wt/1.52 reset --hard HEAD~1` before the retry's attempt commits. The log renames: rename back. The retry: none once an attempt has run; before it, let the task run.

## #033-task-1.53-the-1.46-pin-at-a-run-end-yields (2026-09-21, principal, during the r7 chain; STUCK 1.53 after two attempts lost to the session limit and one stopped, correctly, on a task-text conflict; manual procedure, audited here; no planning commit)

- **What happened.** `1.53` attempt 1 (opus) was cut by the subscription's session limit at turn 39 (`"You've hit your session limit · resets 12pm (UTC)"`, the boundary rule and its matrix written and recovered by the runner's `wip(1.53): recovery of uncommitted changes`), attempt 2 by the same limit at turn 1 before a stub (`NO-JOURNAL 1.53`, the harness's, not the agent's) — #027's third and fourth instances; Michael named the reset and the plain restart followed. Attempt 3 (27 turns, exit 0) finished every deliverable and verified once: `innermostAt` gives a boundary between two inline nodes to the node whose marks equal `doc.resolve(pos).marks()` (the doc comment derived from prosemirror-model's `ResolvedPos.marks()`, `grep -c 'marks()' toggle.ts` 6, never a mark-type list); the twelve-tuple matrix, the `link` and `inline_code` ends, 1.52's block-end control and #031 (a)'s one-character run in `toggle.test.ts` (164/164), the reverted rule red on 16 with the escaped-delimiter bytes recorded; `e2e/web/editor-toggle-run-end.spec.ts` green with the DOM anchor exactly `{ text: "beta.", offset: 5 }` and the bytes `Alpha ~~beta.X~~ gamma\n`; `pnpm lint`, `cargo test`, e2e/web 93/93; `pnpm test` **3,753 passed / 1 failed**, no expected fail. The one red: `packages/editor/test/position-map-editor-leg.test.ts:712`, task 1.46's hand-seeded guard `~~a.~~&#x1F600;`, which asserts `toSource` at the boundary after the run's `.` as ch 6 — the column after the closing `~~`, the "later node" answer this task's clause (1) replaces; `$pos.marks()` there is `delete` (inclusive), so the rule answers ch 4 by construction. That file is outside the task's scope sentence and its diff-stat clause while the same acceptance requires the suite green: the agent stopped and wrote three readings, probing (i) — the pin's column becomes `"~~a.".length` — green (the leg file 256/256, the suite 3,754/3,754 by its count) and reverting it. One narrowing it made inside its scope, recorded in its journal and a backlog line: the three punctuation-edge *start* tuples cannot agree byte-for-byte in the two views at any column (the rendered view keeps the run by encoding the typed letter, `Alpha &#x58;*(beta.)* gamma`, the 1.45/1.49 rule; the raw source keystroke `X*(` is not left-flanking, CommonMark §6.2, and un-forms it), so those three pin each view's bytes and the shared semantics (the letter unmarked, no run holding it).
- **Decision (reading (i); the pin yields, one line, named).** The 1.46 guard pinned the boundary rule of its day at exactly the position L6 re-decides; a pin of the old answer is not the invariant (the neighbour is one reference, `unresolved` empty, the positions inside the neighbour monotone — all unchanged). 1.53 edits `position-map-editor-leg.test.ts` for that one assertion only: line 712's expected column becomes `"~~a.".length`, its comment saying the boundary belongs to the run per `$pos.marks()` (DECISIONS #review-1-r6 L6, #033); nothing else in the file. The scope sentence and the diff-stat clause read, for 1.53, with that file added for that one line — #026 Decision 1's shape (the task whose deliverable changes a rule updates the one out-of-scope assertion that encoded the old rule), inside #028's limit (no other defect, no other file). The narrowing on the three start tuples stands as the agent wrote it: the acceptance's "two-view byte agreement" holds where CommonMark lets both views write the same bytes, and reads as the marks agreement where it cannot — the disagreement is the encoding rule's, not the map's; its `[1.53, a raw source keystroke before a punctuation-edged run]` backlog line stands with its trigger (the r7 reconciliation reads it).
- **Instrument.** This entry stacked on `handoff/027`. One `chore(1.53): principal lesson` commit on `task/1.53` (`docs/lessons.md` only): the retry — FIRST a NEW `- [1.53] ` journal line by one `printf`; the one-line edit at line 712 with its comment; nothing else; the suite ONCE (`pnpm lint && pnpm test && cargo test`, `playwright install chromium`, then e2e/web); complete the stub naming the pin and this entry; commit; DONE. The three logs moved aside as `.evidence/tasks/1.53/{1.a1-429,2.a2-429,3.a3-blocked}.log` (#027). `ralph/ralph.sh retry 1.53` (`blocked → pending`, attempts 0), the restart, a fresh watcher.

## #034-task-1.54-the-two-press-forms (2026-09-21, principal, during the r7 chain; two sonnet attempts capped at 51 — the second without its own journal line, `NO-JOURNAL 1.54` — with the work built on the branch; an orientation before attempt 3, no planning commit)

- **What is on the branch** (`task/1.54`, two runner recovery commits): every `press(page, key, times)` helper under `e2e/web/` (twelve specs, the eight named plus the four the r7 chain added) rewritten, and `expectTextMatchesEditorIgnoringWhitespace` — the text values joined with every ASCII whitespace run removed against the block's `textContent` with the same removal — added to every corpus caller of `editor-fixed-point.test.ts` (nine call sites beside the two exact legs). Neither attempt reached the one suite run, the mutations or its own journal line (attempt 2 completed nothing and stubbed nothing — the `NO-JOURNAL`).
- **The helper's shape, read.** Attempt 1 wrote "settle": after each press, poll until two consecutive `caret(page)` reads agree. That form was chosen for the four vertical sites the specs press more than once so the count does not depend on the starting line (`editor-break-in-mark.spec.ts:162`, `editor-link-edge.spec.ts:179,199`, `editor-trailing-break.spec.ts:106`), where a second `ArrowDown` at the last line legitimately leaves the caret in place and "differs" would time out. But for the counted horizontal arrows — the a1 failure class L7 names — "settle" does not synchronise: two reads taken before Blink has processed the key agree on the old position and the next key fires blind, the same race as before. The task text's sentence is the one that binds: "waits for the DOM caret to move after each press … poll it to differ afterwards".
- **Decision (two forms, stated once).** The helper has two forms, both in every spec that needs them, documented once in the helper's doc comment: (1) `press(page, key, times)` for a counted horizontal arrow polls, after each press, for `caret(page)` to differ from its reading taken before that press (the anchor text or the offset; a count crossing a node boundary is covered by "differs"); (2) the four vertical sites use an edge form (`pressToEdge`, or `press` with a named flag) that keeps attempt 1's settle loop and is followed by the spec's existing anchor assertion (#024's route already asserts the anchor after every vertical motion). No other spec change. The able-to-fail: a scratch copy of one spec with form (1)'s poll removed and a `times` one too many turns its location assertion red as a1 did (the received offset recorded), deleted in the same tool call.
- **Instrument.** This entry stacked on `handoff/027`; one `chore(1.54): principal lesson` commit on `task/1.54` (`docs/lessons.md` only) carrying the decision and the remainder — a NEW `- [1.54] ` journal line by one `printf` FIRST; form (1) and (2) as above; the whitespace-removed assertion's one recorded run over the corpus (it is already in every caller: list the callers, run `pnpm test` once, record any red per #review-1-r6 L8's rule, and its mutation on `astral-between-runs.md` with 1.49's third form disabled and restored); the suite ONCE; complete the stub; commit; DONE. The plain restart (the task is `running`; the second attempt's stop is `NO-JOURNAL`, not `STUCK`); the logs stay in place. Attempt 3 is the last before `STUCK`; at `STUCK` the retry-with-a-lesson shape, the logs moved aside as `{1.a1-capped,2.a2-capped-nostub,3.a3-<word>}.log`.
- **Reversal.** This entry: `git revert` on `handoff/027`. The lesson commit: `git -C .wt/1.54 reset --hard HEAD~1` before attempt 3 commits.

Bytes for every text and attention case are identical to today's (the 1.45 and 1.49 guards green); the verbatim leaves are now untouched. M2: in the same walk, a `break` child whose next sibling is `html` and whose output the assembler rewrote is written as its own `\`+LF when the pair reparses to itself — decided by the round trip (`parse` of the pair holds one paragraph with a `break` directly followed by an `html`; `format.ts` imports `parse` from `./parse.js`, which imports nothing from `format.ts`), never by a hand list of tag names (lesson [1.10.r6d]) — and as one space with no backslash otherwise: an html value that can start an html block (CommonMark §4.6 conditions 1–6: `<div>`, `<!-- -->`, `<script>`, `<?…?>`, `<![CDATA[`) would turn the next line into a block, so the break is lost there, a documented loss on the `[#030 product]` backlog line, never a stray byte. ## #037-gate-1.verify.r8h-widget-boundary-caret-spelling (2026-09-22, plan.1.verify.r8h.r0; principal, from the run's log and the installed prosemirror-view; scope per DECISIONS #019, #021, #022, #024, #035, #036, handoff 029)

- **What happened.** `scripts/gate.sh 1.verify.r8h` a1 (ci.yml run 35755695990 at `b7f45a5`, Michael's shell): every unit test file, `cargo test` and the lint step green on all three runners; macos-latest 98/98 Playwright cases; **ubuntu-latest 97 passed, 1 failed; windows-latest 96 passed, 2 failed**. Both failures are at the click that seeds the caret, before the toggle chord runs: (1) `e2e/web/editor-toggle-code-span-end.spec.ts:127` (task 1.60), ubuntu and windows — expected `{ text: "foo", offset: 3 }`, received `{ text: null, offset: 4 }`; (2) `e2e/web/editor-toggle-cell-code-pipe.spec.ts:167` in `seedWithCaretAfterA` (task 1.59), windows only — expected `{ text: "a|b", offset: 0 }`, received `{ text: null, offset: 0 }`. `GATE-FAILED`, `1.verify.r8h` → `blocked`, plan request `plan.1.verify.r8h.r0`. Artifacts fetched only on success (#020); `workflow.log` is the evidence. Both specs are new in the r8 chain and 1.60 changed editor runtime (`toggle.ts`, `DevEditor.tsx`), so #023's rerun criterion does not apply; the product path is not what failed — neither case reached the chord.
- **Cause, read in the installed package** (`node_modules/.pnpm/prosemirror-view@1.42.3/node_modules/prosemirror-view/dist/index.js`, not from memory). A caret at either edge of a code span sits at a reveal-widget boundary: task 1.4's `.essaydown-delimiter` backticks are widget decorations (`packages/editor/src/reveal.ts`; the opening delimiter has a positive `side`, the closing one a negative `side`) and prosemirror-view gives every widget `contentEditable = "false"` (line 1173). One document position there has two DOM spellings. Blink's hit test anchors the selection in the `<code>` text node (`foo`/3, `a|b`/0). ProseMirror's own `NodeViewDesc.domFromPos` (line 922) spells the same position on the parent element: after a closing widget it returns `{ node: contentDOM, offset: domIndex(widget) + 1 }` — `<p>` at 4 for `see `foo`` (text, open widget, `<code>`, close widget), exactly the value both runners received — and before an opening widget (the "go back" loop over `side >= 0` widgets) `{ node: contentDOM, offset: 0 }` — the `<td>` at 0 the windows run received, and the spelling the pipe spec's second case already asserts after the toggle back, where ProseMirror wrote the DOM. `scanFor` returns false at a `contentEditable == "false"` child, so the two spellings are not equivalent to `setSelection` (line 1025), which re-spells the DOM whenever they differ; `handlers.focus` (line 3904) schedules a `selectionToDOM` 20 ms after the editor gains focus when the observer's `currentSelection` differs from the DOM's. Which spelling a poll reads therefore depends on whether the view re-synced after the click — a race on a 20 ms timer and the observer's flush, which the 5 s poll cannot settle because both spellings are stable, and which the container (green at 1.59 and 1.60) and macos-latest resolved one way and ubuntu-latest and windows-latest the other. Both spellings resolve to the same document position; the precondition asserted a DOM spelling that belongs to whichever of Blink and ProseMirror wrote the DOM last, while what the toggle reads is the editor's selection (`toggle` in `DevEditor.tsx` hands `pm.state.selection.head` and `pm.state.storedMarks` to `toSource`).
- **Scope decision (explicit, so the fix task does not pick).** Candidates: (1) a rerun — no: #023 needs an unchanged spec and no editor runtime change, and the spelling is deterministic on two runners for case (1); (2) accept both spellings in `caret` — a hedge over two agents' output, and the pipe spec's `ArrowRight` after a boundary click would step over the widget rather than into the text, so its arrow route is broken as well; (3) resolve the DOM anchor through `dom.pmViewDesc.posFromDOM` in the page — test-only, but an undocumented property, and it yields no marks; (4) **a dev-bar selection readout**: `data-testid="selection"` on `/dev/editor`, written by a ProseMirror plugin view on every `updateState` (so the store pulls that bypass `dispatchTransaction` count), holding `{ before, empty, marks, stored }` — the text of the caret's textblock before the caret, the selection's emptiness, `$head.marks()` and `storedMarks` by type name — which is the editor's selection as `toSource` and the two-view guard (#review-1-r7 M5) receive it, through public API, at no React state per transaction; and for the pipe spec a click at an interior text position (the left edge of the pipe, measured from the text node's own `Range` rect), which has one DOM spelling, with the arrow removed. Chosen: (4). The precondition is not dropped (#024): `before: "see foo"` proves the span's end byte for byte and `marks: ["inline_code"]` with `stored: null` is M5's click route stated positively. The 200 ms tick stays; its owner is the `[1.46, found outside scope]` line, and a task that removes it is a Phase 2 decision. A reviewer reading this: the readout is a dev-route test handle beside `copy-markdown` and `fixture-file`, not product code, and `toggle` is unchanged.
- **Graph change (RUNNER-SPEC §2 rule (a)), both physically in PRD §8 and `ralph/tasks.json`.** Appended after `1.verify.r8`: **`1.62`** (sonnet; `apps/desktop/src/dev/DevEditor.tsx` for the readout, the two spec files, one backlog evidence line; depends on `1.verify.r8h`) and **`1.verify.r8.g1`** (sonnet; `needsCI`, `refTemplate ci/1.verify.r8.g1/a{n}`, `artifactNames` unchanged; depends on `1.62`; the promote-lessons step of `1.verify.r8` is integrated at `b7f45a5` and is not repeated; `ciAcceptance` names `/logs/ci/1.verify.r8.g1h/accepted/`). `1.10.r8a/b/c` now depend on `1.verify.r8.g1` (the generator makes that `1.verify.r8.g1h`) and their `/logs/ci` reference reads `1.verify.r8.g1h`; otherwise unchanged. `ralph/EXPECTED_COUNT` 261 → 264 (210 raw + 21 review-set + 33 CI gates). `1.verify.r8h` becomes `superseded` at `sync-state`.
- **Model choice.** `1.62` is sonnet for #036's reason: the route is specified to the file and the value, and this time the container can run both specs (it reproduces the class or not, and records which); the only unobservable is the runner. `1.verify.r8.g1` is sonnet, as every verifier.
- **Acceptance tails.** `1.62`'s acceptance names file-level greps on the three files and the latency spec by name, never the suite's total (#032's lesson, #review-1-r7).
- **For the reconciliation `1.10.r8d`.** The implementation SHA is `1.verify.r8.g1`'s integrated commit, not `b7f45a5`. This entry lands in the tree first; `handoff/029` holds only `docs/handoffs/`, so `git cherry-pick phase/1..handoff/029` has no DECISIONS.md tail to conflict on. `1.62`'s reproduction record (whether the container's Chrome shows the re-spelling) is evidence for the backlog line, not a decision.
- **Reversal.** Before this planning commit integrates: `git -C .wt/plan.1.verify.r8h.r0 reset --hard phase/1` (the branch back to its cut at `b7f45a5`); the request stays `running` (git-only, as #018–#024, #035, #036). After integration a plan request has no undo: `1.verify.r8h` stays `superseded` and any further change is another planning commit.
